## 48. When LLMs Manipulate: Measuring Behavioral, Social, and Temporal Effects

**中文标题**: When LLMs Manipulate: Measuring Behavioral, Social, and Temporal Effects

**作者**: Sadia Asif, Israel Antonio Rosales Laguan, Shumaila Asif, Haris Khan, Muneeb Asif et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27573v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27573v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27573v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.CY, cs.HC, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Large language models (LLMs) can influence user trust and decision-making through subtle linguistic cues, yet the behavioral effects of manipulative outputs remain poorly quantified. We introduce a behavioral computing framework for measuring \textit{behavioral vulnerability}, defined as users' susceptibility to manipulative AI responses. Building on DarkPatterns-LLM benchmark, we generate controlled manipulative stimuli using Manipulation Gradient Descent (MGD), targeting biases associated with authority, urgency, and social proof. We analyze participant responses using the Multi-Stakeholder Impact Assessment Network (MSIAN) to model cross-user influence and the Temporal Harm Propagation Model (THPM) to characterize the persistence of behavioral effects over time. In a study involving 120 participants, manipulative framing produced significant changes in trust calibration and compliance ($p < 0.01$), with effects also propagating across social contexts. These findings highlight the need to extend LLM safety evaluation beyond model outputs to measurable behavioral consequences and provide a computational framework for quantifying individual, social, and temporal risks arising from manipulative AI interactions.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Large language models (LLMs) can influence user trust and decision-making through subtle linguistic cues, yet the behavioral effects of manipulative outputs remain poorly quantified. We introduce a behavioral computing framework for measuring \textit{behavioral vulnerability}, defined as users' susceptibility to manipulative AI responses. Building on DarkPatterns-LLM benchmark, we generate controlled manipulative stimuli using Manipulation Gradient Descent (MGD), targeting biases associated with authority, urgency, and social proof. We analyze participant responses using the Multi-Stakeholder Impact Assessment Network (MSIAN) to model cross-user influence and the Temporal Harm Propagation Model (THPM) to characterize the persistence of behavioral effects over time. In a study involving 120 participants, manipulative framing produced significant changes in trust calibration and compliance ($p < 0.01$), with effects also propagating across social contexts. These findings highlight the need to extend LLM safety evaluation beyond model outputs to measurable behavioral consequences and provide a computational framework for quantifying individual, social, and temporal risks arising from manipulative AI interactions.

</details>

## 49. AnchorRM: Parallel Intra-Batch State Reconstruction with Anchor-Based Residual Memory for Temporal Graph Neural Networks

**中文标题**: AnchorRM: Parallel Intra-Batch State Reconstruction with Anchor-Based Residual Memory for Temporal Graph Neural Networks

**作者**: Haroon Gharwi, Kai Shu, Yue Dai

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27558v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27558v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27558v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Memory-based temporal graph neural networks (TGN) rely on recurrent node memories to capture evolving interaction histories. However, temporal mini-batching introduces a trade-off between computational efficiency and temporal fidelity: when a node appears multiple times within the same batch, later interactions are evaluated using stale pre-batch memory because the intermediate memory updates have not yet been committed. This problem becomes more severe as temporal batch size increases. To overcome this challenge, we propose AnchorRM, an anchor-based residual memory mechanism that reconstructs event-specific intra-batch node states without executing sequential recurrent updates. AnchorRM treats the last committed node memory at the beginning of each batch as a pre-batch fixed anchor and uses causally preceding same-batch interactions to infer the missing state evolution relative to that anchor. The learned residual then calibrates the anchor to reconstruct an event-specific node memory for each interaction. Because each event-specific residual is predicted directly from the fixed anchor and its causal history, reconstructed states do not depend on previously reconstructed states and can therefore be computed in parallel within the batch while preserving temporal causality. Experiments across four temporal graph datasets and three memory-based backbones demonstrate that AnchorRM achieves an average 6.16x training speedup (up to 10.05x) and 28.67x batch scaling (up to 40x). These results demonstrate that AnchorRM substantially improves the scalability and training efficiency of memory-based temporal graph learning while maintaining competitive predictive performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Memory-based temporal graph neural networks (TGN) rely on recurrent node memories to capture evolving interaction histories. However, temporal mini-batching introduces a trade-off between computational efficiency and temporal fidelity: when a node appears multiple times within the same batch, later interactions are evaluated using stale pre-batch memory because the intermediate memory updates have not yet been committed. This problem becomes more severe as temporal batch size increases. To overcome this challenge, we propose AnchorRM, an anchor-based residual memory mechanism that reconstructs event-specific intra-batch node states without executing sequential recurrent updates. AnchorRM treats the last committed node memory at the beginning of each batch as a pre-batch fixed anchor and uses causally preceding same-batch interactions to infer the missing state evolution relative to that anchor. The learned residual then calibrates the anchor to reconstruct an event-specific node memory for each interaction. Because each event-specific residual is predicted directly from the fixed anchor and its causal history, reconstructed states do not depend on previously reconstructed states and can therefore be computed in parallel within the batch while preserving temporal causality. Experiments across four temporal graph datasets and three memory-based backbones demonstrate that AnchorRM achieves an average 6.16x training speedup (up to 10.05x) and 28.67x batch scaling (up to 40x). These results demonstrate that AnchorRM substantially improves the scalability and training efficiency of memory-based temporal graph learning while maintaining competitive predictive performance.

</details>

## 50. MemDev-Bench: Does Memory Help Code Agents?Benchmarking Memory Systems over Real Repository Histories

**中文标题**: MemDev-Bench: Does Memory Help Code Agents?Benchmarking Memory Systems over Real Repository Histories

**作者**: Guorui Chen, Tao Liu, Zhenzhen Huang, Yidan Zhang, Liyu Jiang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27524v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27524v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27524v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Code agents increasingly work on evolving real repositories, and memory systems aim to help them reuse earlier experience. However, such experience is diverse and evolving, so it remains unclear how memory systems organize it and thereby affect code agents. Existing benchmarks cannot answer this reliably because they lack either temporally valid histories or control over prior-task relevance. In this paper, we present MemDev-Bench to evaluate how memory systems affect code agents over diverse repository histories. It pairs each task with relevance-annotated earlier tasks from the same repository to build histories with controlled relevance. We also build an automated pipeline that reliably makes these earlier tasks executable and verifiable, producing 4,869 historical tasks for 555 base tasks. Based on MemDev-Bench, our systematic evaluation of memory systems across diverse development scenarios yields three main findings. (1) Memory mainly improves fine-grained localization and editing, increasing functional correctness. (2) Memory also causes failures on otherwise solvable tasks even with related history, and unrelated history tends to increase such failures. These failures mainly stem from agents reusing memory without reassessing its applicability. (3) Compared with using memory systems, directly providing agents with raw historical trajectories substantially reduces their steps. Further analysis suggests that agents save these steps by reusing process information that compressed memories tend to omit. These results suggest that future memory systems should preserve process information and reassess memory applicability against current task context.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Code agents increasingly work on evolving real repositories, and memory systems aim to help them reuse earlier experience. However, such experience is diverse and evolving, so it remains unclear how memory systems organize it and thereby affect code agents. Existing benchmarks cannot answer this reliably because they lack either temporally valid histories or control over prior-task relevance. In this paper, we present MemDev-Bench to evaluate how memory systems affect code agents over diverse repository histories. It pairs each task with relevance-annotated earlier tasks from the same repository to build histories with controlled relevance. We also build an automated pipeline that reliably makes these earlier tasks executable and verifiable, producing 4,869 historical tasks for 555 base tasks. Based on MemDev-Bench, our systematic evaluation of memory systems across diverse development scenarios yields three main findings. (1) Memory mainly improves fine-grained localization and editing, increasing functional correctness. (2) Memory also causes failures on otherwise solvable tasks even with related history, and unrelated history tends to increase such failures. These failures mainly stem from agents reusing memory without reassessing its applicability. (3) Compared with using memory systems, directly providing agents with raw historical trajectories substantially reduces their steps. Further analysis suggests that agents save these steps by reusing process information that compressed memories tend to omit. These results suggest that future memory systems should preserve process information and reassess memory applicability against current task context.

</details>

## 51. Physical Canvas: A Vision Tactile World Model For Robot Policy Evaluation And Learning

**中文标题**: Physical Canvas: A Vision Tactile World Model For Robot Policy Evaluation And Learning

**作者**: Zebin Xing, Yuhang Zheng, Yupeng Zheng, Yichen Zhang, Zichen Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28613v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28613v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28613v1)

**项目主页**: https://zebinx.github.io/PhysCanvas_HomePage/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Evaluating and post-training robot policies through physical interaction is costly and difficult to scale, motivating action-conditioned world models as interactive simulators. However, visually plausible predictions can misrepresent contact dynamics, providing unreliable feedback for policy evaluation and learning. We introduce PhysCanvas, a state-consistent visuo-tactile world model for contact-rich robot manipulation. PhysCanvas unifies multi-view RGB, tactile images, marker displacement, task state, and actions within a unified vision space. All modalities share a video VAE and pretrained generative backbone, allowing optical touch to reuse visual and temporal priors. Motion images derived through forward kinematics and camera projection provide spatially aligned action conditioning, while an initial RGB anchor and updated reference observations guide sequential generation. Our consistency-aware post-training uses the frozen Scene Reward Model, Modality Reward Model, and State Reward Model to supervise scene preservation, visual-tactile compatibility, and agreement with real task outcomes. The resulting world model supports closed-loop policy rollouts for evaluation and provides imagined interactions and rewards for GRPO policy updates. Across four real-world manipulation tasks, when policies are rolled out in the world model, State Reward Model supervision increases the arithmetic mean of task-wise Pearson correlations between world-model and real-world success rates from 0.602 to 0.885. Policy post-training in the reward-guided world model further increases $π_{0.5}$'s mean real-world success rate from a 59.1% SFT baseline to 70.8%. These results demonstrate the value of unified multimodal modeling and consistency-aware post-training for robot policy evaluation and learning. Project page: https://zebinx.github.io/PhysCanvas_HomePage/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Evaluating and post-training robot policies through physical interaction is costly and difficult to scale, motivating action-conditioned world models as interactive simulators. However, visually plausible predictions can misrepresent contact dynamics, providing unreliable feedback for policy evaluation and learning. The resulting world model supports closed-loop policy rollouts for evaluation and provides imagined interactions and rewards for GRPO policy updates. Across four real-world manipulation tasks, when policies are rolled out in the world model, State Reward Model supervision increases the arithmetic mean of task-wise Pearson correlations between world-model and real-world success rates from 0.602 to 0.885. Policy post-training in the reward-guided world model further increases $π_{0.5}$'s mean real-world success rate from a 59.1% SFT baseline to 70.8%. These results demonstrate the value of unified multimodal modeling and consistency-aware post-training for robot policy evaluation and learning. Project page: https://zebinx.github.io/PhysCanvas_HomePage/.

</details>

## 52. From Predictive Geometry to Action: Learning Complementary Predictive Representations for Embodied Manipulation

**中文标题**: From Predictive Geometry to Action: Learning Complementary Predictive Representations for Embodied Manipulation

**作者**: Xiaoxu Xu, Hao Li, Hongtao Zhang, Yili Tang, Jiafei Cao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28164v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28164v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28164v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/xuxiaoxxxx/CPR.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Effective manipulation requires a policy to capture not only about the current scene geometry, but also about how that geometry will evolve under interaction. Existing approaches either augment policies with geometric information from current observations or introduce future prediction as auxiliary supervision, leaving open how predictive geometry should be represented for action generation. Therefore, we propose a two-stage framework, which learns complementary predictive representations from multi-view video clips. During pretraining, a frozen 3D foundation model provides dense geometric features that are compressed into predictive geometric representations and optimized to preserve scene structure and its temporal evolution. Action supervision is then applied to geometric transitions to extract a complementary motor representation that emphasizes action-relevant transitions. During downstream post-training, predictive geometry is transferred through geometry queries, while the motor representation guides intermediate features along the action-generation pathway. Future observations are used only as privileged supervision during training and are not required at inference time. Across LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, SimplerEnv, and real-world bimanual and single-arm tasks, our approach consistently improves downstream policies, including an absolute gain of 10.4 percentage points on the WidowX evaluation. We further demonstrate transfer across multiple VLA architectures and WAMs, with improvements in both future-world and action prediction. The code will be available at https://github.com/xuxiaoxxxx/CPR.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Therefore, we propose a two-stage framework, which learns complementary predictive representations from multi-view video clips. During pretraining, a frozen 3D foundation model provides dense geometric features that are compressed into predictive geometric representations and optimized to preserve scene structure and its temporal evolution. Action supervision is then applied to geometric transitions to extract a complementary motor representation that emphasizes action-relevant transitions. During downstream post-training, predictive geometry is transferred through geometry queries, while the motor representation guides intermediate features along the action-generation pathway. Future observations are used only as privileged supervision during training and are not required at inference time. Across LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, SimplerEnv, and real-world bimanual and single-arm tasks, our approach consistently improves downstream policies, including an absolute gain of 10.4 percentage points on the WidowX evaluation. We further demonstrate transfer across multiple VLA architectures and WAMs, with improvements in both future-world and action prediction. The code will be available at https://github.com/xuxiaoxxxx/CPR.

</details>

## 53. Temporal Difference Learning of Probabilistic Reach-Avoid Value Functions

**中文标题**: Temporal Difference Learning of Probabilistic Reach-Avoid Value Functions

**作者**: Elias Fontanari, Pietro Noah Crestaz, Matteo Saveriano, Andrea Del Prete

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28106v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28106v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28106v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, eess.SY

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reach-avoid sets, defining the states from which a task can be completed while avoiding unsafe regions, are fundamental tools for deploying robots in environments subject to disturbances and uncertainty, as well as for runtime task-feasibility monitoring, allowing prediction of whether a state is safe and will lead to successful task completion. Classical Hamilton-Jacobi Reachability (HJR) and its recent neural extensions (e.g., Lipschitz Continuous Reachability Learning, DeepReach) treat disturbances adversarially, computing the reach-avoid set under worst-case disturbances from a bounded set. This guarantees safety but can yield overly conservative sets when disturbances are stochastic rather than adversarial, and additionally requires the disturbance set to be known or specified in advance.   We propose Probabilistic reach-avoid Value Function (PVF), a self-supervised method, applicable to black-box systems, for computing reach-avoid sets as the states with a certain reach-avoid probability under stochastic, potentially unbounded disturbances. We evaluate our approach against HJR-based baselines on systems of increasing complexity, up to a Hopper locomotion task, and show that our probabilistic sets are less conservative and more accurate than adversarial HJR sets when disturbances are non-adversarial.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reach-avoid sets, defining the states from which a task can be completed while avoiding unsafe regions, are fundamental tools for deploying robots in environments subject to disturbances and uncertainty, as well as for runtime task-feasibility monitoring, allowing prediction of whether a state is safe and will lead to successful task completion. Classical Hamilton-Jacobi Reachability (HJR) and its recent neural extensions (e.g., Lipschitz Continuous Reachability Learning, DeepReach) treat disturbances adversarially, computing the reach-avoid set under worst-case disturbances from a bounded set. This guarantees safety but can yield overly conservative sets when disturbances are stochastic rather than adversarial, and additionally requires the disturbance set to be known or specified in advance.   We propose Probabilistic reach-avoid Value Function (PVF), a self-supervised method, applicable to black-box systems, for computing reach-avoid sets as the states with a certain reach-avoid probability under stochastic, potentially unbounded disturbances. We evaluate our approach against HJR-based baselines on systems of increasing complexity, up to a Hopper locomotion task, and show that our probabilistic sets are less conservative and more accurate than adversarial HJR sets when disturbances are non-adversarial.

</details>

## 54. EchoNav: Evidential BEV Completion from Historical Observations for Visual Navigation

**中文标题**: EchoNav: Evidential BEV Completion from Historical Observations for Visual Navigation

**作者**: Diwen Liu, Yiyang Shao, Borui Wang, Chi Zhang, Yunhui Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27616v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27616v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27616v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Visual navigation in cluttered environments requires robots to recover previously observed structure and infer obstacles and free space that remain occluded. We present a framework that combines temporal aggregation and occupancy completion in an evidential bird's-eye-view (BEV) representation predicted from short RGB sequences. A decoder adapts frozen VGGT-Omega features and combines historical residual proposals using cellwise weights and a learned no-update option. The BEV predictor produces a local map in the latest ego frame without depth or camera-pose inputs or explicit geometric warping. The evidential model treats observed free space separately and predicts occupancy with associated confidence across the remaining supported region. We train the predictor on simulated RGB sequences paired with complete and visibility-masked occupancy annotations. Our experiments evaluate navigation in simulation and on a wheeled robot, with additional demonstrations on quadruped and humanoid robots. In simulated sequential two-goal navigation, our system achieves 89.6% success, outperforming the strongest evaluated baseline by 32.5 percentage points. Across 60 wheeled-robot trials in 20 scenes, it achieves 73.3% success, compared with 33.3% for the strongest evaluated baseline.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Visual navigation in cluttered environments requires robots to recover previously observed structure and infer obstacles and free space that remain occluded. We present a framework that combines temporal aggregation and occupancy completion in an evidential bird's-eye-view (BEV) representation predicted from short RGB sequences. A decoder adapts frozen VGGT-Omega features and combines historical residual proposals using cellwise weights and a learned no-update option. The BEV predictor produces a local map in the latest ego frame without depth or camera-pose inputs or explicit geometric warping. The evidential model treats observed free space separately and predicts occupancy with associated confidence across the remaining supported region. We train the predictor on simulated RGB sequences paired with complete and visibility-masked occupancy annotations. Our experiments evaluate navigation in simulation and on a wheeled robot, with additional demonstrations on quadruped and humanoid robots. In simulated sequential two-goal navigation, our system achieves 89.6% success, outperforming the strongest evaluated baseline by 32.5 percentage points. Across 60 wheeled-robot trials in 20 scenes, it achieves 73.3% success, compared with 33.3% for the strongest evaluated baseline.

</details>

## 55. PaPi: Parallel Picard-Duhamel Integration for Fast Neuro-Spectral Architectures

**中文标题**: PaPi: Parallel Picard-Duhamel Integration for Fast Neuro-Spectral Architectures

**作者**: Arthur Bizzi, Márcio Marques, Kleyton Da Costa, Vitor Balestro, Tiago Novello et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28591v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28591v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28591v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Neuro-spectral architectures (NeuSA) are a class of physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) that have proven effective for solving time-dependent PDEs in the frequency domain. However, training and inference often rely on costly numerical integration of the learned vector field, typically using sequential Runge-Kutta time stepping, which limits temporal parallelism. We introduce PaPi, an integration scheme inspired by weakly nonlinear flows that enables time-Parallel inference through exponential integration and a Duhamel-Picard scheme. We show that the Duhamel-Picard iteration converges exponentially for all flows, and provide explicit error bounds that depend on how close the flow is to linear. Experiments show that PaPi accelerates training and inference by up to two orders of magnitude, achieving training times of under one second for low-dimensional PDE learning problems, while maintaining competitive predictive accuracy.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Neuro-spectral architectures (NeuSA) are a class of physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) that have proven effective for solving time-dependent PDEs in the frequency domain. However, training and inference often rely on costly numerical integration of the learned vector field, typically using sequential Runge-Kutta time stepping, which limits temporal parallelism. We introduce PaPi, an integration scheme inspired by weakly nonlinear flows that enables time-Parallel inference through exponential integration and a Duhamel-Picard scheme. We show that the Duhamel-Picard iteration converges exponentially for all flows, and provide explicit error bounds that depend on how close the flow is to linear. Experiments show that PaPi accelerates training and inference by up to two orders of magnitude, achieving training times of under one second for low-dimensional PDE learning problems, while maintaining competitive predictive accuracy.

</details>

## 56. GraphCast-Mamba: Learning Non-Markovian Corrections for Weather Forecasting

**中文标题**: GraphCast-Mamba: Learning Non-Markovian Corrections for Weather Forecasting

**作者**: Ilya Vilkoviskiy, Liang-Hong Mo, Suozhi Huang, Dmitry A. Abanin

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28512v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28512v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28512v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Unresolved atmospheric processes can introduce history dependence beyond the short input windows used by many weather forecasting models. We introduce GraphCast--Mamba, which augments a frozen, pretrained $1^\circ$ GraphCast backbone with a trainable Mamba correction branch combining graph message passing and Mamba layers. The branch retains a temporal memory state across successive six-hour forecast steps, learning corrections informed by temporal context beyond GraphCast's two-frame input. Training is confined to the correction branch, using ERA5 data from 2015--2021. After 2,500 optimizer updates, including warm-up, the selected model achieves substantial improvements relative to the uncorrected GraphCast backbone. It reduces the GraphCast loss (normalized weighted mean-squared error; MSE) by 14.42% when aggregated over the full ten-day forecast and by 21.66% at day 10 (240 h after forecast initialization). At this final forecast lead time, root-mean-square error (RMSE) decreases by 7.09--14.60% across all 11 evaluated variable groups, spanning temperature, wind, pressure, geopotential, specific humidity, and precipitation. These results demonstrate broad improvements in ten-day weather forecasts through a short correction-training schedule while retaining the pretrained backbone.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Unresolved atmospheric processes can introduce history dependence beyond the short input windows used by many weather forecasting models. We introduce GraphCast--Mamba, which augments a frozen, pretrained $1^\circ$ GraphCast backbone with a trainable Mamba correction branch combining graph message passing and Mamba layers. The branch retains a temporal memory state across successive six-hour forecast steps, learning corrections informed by temporal context beyond GraphCast's two-frame input. Training is confined to the correction branch, using ERA5 data from 2015--2021. After 2,500 optimizer updates, including warm-up, the selected model achieves substantial improvements relative to the uncorrected GraphCast backbone. It reduces the GraphCast loss (normalized weighted mean-squared error; MSE) by 14.42% when aggregated over the full ten-day forecast and by 21.66% at day 10 (240 h after forecast initialization). At this final forecast lead time, root-mean-square error (RMSE) decreases by 7.09--14.60% across all 11 evaluated variable groups, spanning temperature, wind, pressure, geopotential, specific humidity, and precipitation. These results demonstrate broad improvements in ten-day weather forecasts through a short correction-training schedule while retaining the pretrained backbone.

</details>

## 57. Geometry-Agnostic Data-Driven Discovery of Extreme Scenarios

**中文标题**: Geometry-Agnostic Data-Driven Discovery of Extreme Scenarios

**作者**: Angeline Hu, Ava K. Tse, Kaitlyn S. Yasumura, Luciana Sarabia, Malcolm Yeung et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28487v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28487v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28487v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, math-ph

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We introduce a two-stage data-driven neural framework for discovering dynamically admissible extreme scenarios. Rather than assuming prior knowledge of the governing equations, the first stage infers the effective dynamical constraints directly from observational data. When the underlying physical system possesses suitable symmetries, the learned dynamics can be represented in a geometry-agnostic form, independent of any fixed grid, coordinate system, dimensionality, and intrinsic curvature. In the second stage, a physics-informed neural-search uses this learned dynamics to uncover rare, high-impact coherent structures that may not have appeared in the original observations. We demonstrate this framework through representative examples of increasing dimensionality and geometric complexity. Starting from a traditionally learned Duffing dynamics, we find a homoclinic pulse as a plausible localized extreme scenario. Next, since the same equation also appears in the stationary nonlinear Schrodinger system, with the coordinate interpreted as space instead of time, this pulse becomes a standing soliton. We then retrain the first stage using a geometry-agnostic neural representation, enabling the learned dynamics to be lifted to higher-dimensional space to obtain higher-dimensional solitary profile, known as Townes soliton. Finally, with a Liouville system, we demonstrate that dynamics learned from data on a (1+1)-dimensional Lorentzian quasi-manifold can be transferred to a (2+0)-dimensional Euclidean manifold to identify an instanton. Our approach can be applied to other coherent spatiotemporal structures, including kinks, rogue waves, compactons, peakons, and potentially even singularities, with applications in risk analysis, nonlinear forecasting, and anomaly detection.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce a two-stage data-driven neural framework for discovering dynamically admissible extreme scenarios. Rather than assuming prior knowledge of the governing equations, the first stage infers the effective dynamical constraints directly from observational data. When the underlying physical system possesses suitable symmetries, the learned dynamics can be represented in a geometry-agnostic form, independent of any fixed grid, coordinate system, dimensionality, and intrinsic curvature. In the second stage, a physics-informed neural-search uses this learned dynamics to uncover rare, high-impact coherent structures that may not have appeared in the original observations. We demonstrate this framework through representative examples of increasing dimensionality and geometric complexity. Starting from a traditionally learned Duffing dynamics, we find a homoclinic pulse as a plausible localized extreme scenario. Next, since the same equation also appears in the stationary nonlinear Schrodinger system, with the coordinate interpreted as space instead of time, this pulse becomes a standing soliton. We then retrain the first stage using a geometry-agnostic neural representation, enabling the learned dynamics to be lifted to higher-dimensional space to obtain higher-dimensional solitary profile, known as Townes soliton. Finally, with a Liouville system, we demonstrate that dynamics learned from data on a (1+1)-dimensional Lorentzian quasi-manifold can be transferred to a (2+0)-dimensional Euclidean manifold to identify an instanton. Our approach can be applied to other coherent spatiotemporal structures, including kinks, rogue waves, compactons, peakons, and potentially even singularities, with applications in risk analysis, nonlinear forecasting, and anomaly detection.

</details>

## 58. Region-Specific EEG Classification of Hypnotic Susceptibility Using a Sliding-Window 2D CNN: A Subject-Independent Evaluation

**中文标题**: Region-Specific EEG Classification of Hypnotic Susceptibility Using a Sliding-Window 2D CNN: A Subject-Independent Evaluation

**作者**: Krzysztof Wrobel, Grzegorz M. Wojcik

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28427v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28427v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28427v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We investigate whether a 2D Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) trained on minimally preprocessed EEG signals can classify hypnotic susceptibility across distinct brain regions. Sixty-two male participants (32 Control, 30 Guided Imagery / Hipno group) underwent a 20-minute EEG recording using a 256-channel EGI cap. A sliding-window approach evaluates all 10-second windows within the recording for each of seven predefined brain regions (prefrontal, frontal, temporal, central, parietal, occipital, and facial), yielding up to 868 independently trained models. Initial screening with a segment-level 90/10 random split showed that five regions exceeded the 0.67 accuracy threshold, with the occipital region achieving the highest mean accuracy (0.721) across 90.3% of windows. Subject-independent evaluation via Leave-One-Subject-Out (LOSO) cross-validation, however, substantially revised this picture: occipital performance collapsed to near-chance (mean ACC 0.453, AUC 0.429), revealing that high random-split accuracy was driven by subject-specific EEG fingerprints rather than genuine group differences. In contrast, the frontal and prefrontal regions maintained above-chance LOSO accuracy (0.646 and 0.624, respectively), demonstrating genuine generalisability. These results underscore the necessity of subject-independent validation in EEG-based classification studies and highlight anterior cortical regions as more reliable neural markers of hypnotic susceptibility.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Sixty-two male participants (32 Control, 30 Guided Imagery / Hipno group) underwent a 20-minute EEG recording using a 256-channel EGI cap. A sliding-window approach evaluates all 10-second windows within the recording for each of seven predefined brain regions (prefrontal, frontal, temporal, central, parietal, occipital, and facial), yielding up to 868 independently trained models. Initial screening with a segment-level 90/10 random split showed that five regions exceeded the 0.67 accuracy threshold, with the occipital region achieving the highest mean accuracy (0.721) across 90.3% of windows. Subject-independent evaluation via Leave-One-Subject-Out (LOSO) cross-validation, however, substantially revised this picture: occipital performance collapsed to near-chance (mean ACC 0.453, AUC 0.429), revealing that high random-split accuracy was driven by subject-specific EEG fingerprints rather than genuine group differences. In contrast, the frontal and prefrontal regions maintained above-chance LOSO accuracy (0.646 and 0.624, respectively), demonstrating genuine generalisability. These results underscore the necessity of subject-independent validation in EEG-based classification studies and highlight anterior cortical regions as more reliable neural markers of hypnotic susceptibility.

</details>

## 59. TerraNostra-Bench: Benchmarking Spatial Downscaling and Temporal Rollout for Global 1-km Land-Use Reconstruction

**中文标题**: TerraNostra-Bench: Benchmarking Spatial Downscaling and Temporal Rollout for Global 1-km Land-Use Reconstruction

**作者**: Amirpasha Mozaffari, Marina Castaño, Stefano Materia, Joan Vedrí, Jordi Varela-Agrelo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27793v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27793v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27793v1)

**类别**: physics.ao-ph, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Uncertainty in the terrestrial carbon cycle remains a major constraint in climate projections, partly due to imperfect land surface representations in Earth system models. We propose a data-driven framework for high-resolution land-use reconstruction, treated as a semantic-segmentation task, that combines coarse-resolution scenario data and climate classification with static geophysical features to produce annual land-use maps at 1 km resolution. We introduce TerraNostra-Bench, a change-aware evaluation protocol that separates spatial downscaling from autoregressive temporal rollout and scores models on the rare land-use-change pixels rather than the dominant static background. Using it, we compare convolutional (U-Net) and transformer (Swin) backbones in autoregressive and non-autoregressive schemes against persistence, linear, and shallow convolutional baselines. The autoregressive prior is the most informative input yet accumulates error over long rollouts: autoregressive models lose 5.7-7.2 accuracy points over 13 years, against 2.2-2.8 for non-autoregressive models. This trade-off is governed by the autoregressive choice rather than the backbone. It can be tuned by an inference-time prior-trust threshold that requires no retraining and by the training-time weighting of reconstruction and change objectives; the two levers trace complementary frontiers, with calibration recovering up to 10 accuracy points while keeping change sensitivity above the persistence floor. Extrapolating beyond the training window, backward to 1955-1959 and forward to 2015-2019 under scenario forcing, the models retain most of their in-window skill and show the same trade-off, indicating it is intrinsic to the design. This work provides the methodological foundation and evaluation surface for a forthcoming temporally continuous land-use product.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 60. Engram Memory Network: Brain-Inspired Prototype Explanations

**中文标题**: Engram Memory Network: Brain-Inspired Prototype Explanations

**作者**: Hyunjun Kim, Myoung Hoon Ha

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27770v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27770v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27770v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Artificial intelligence systems are increasingly deployed in high-stakes decision-making. In response, prototype-based models in explainable AI have attracted growing attention for their ability to retrieve visually similar examples as faithful explanations. Although these models achieve competitive classification accuracy, they often exhibit degraded explanation quality across datasets with varying characteristics, limiting their practical applicability. We draw on insights from cognitive neuroscience, particularly the human memory system, which encodes input-relevant information into representations that support recognition. We propose an Engram Memory Network (EMN), a model that emulates the ventral visual stream and inferior temporal cortex in the human brain. Central to this model is a Hopfield-inspired memory module that iteratively retrieves representative prototypes. These memory-refined latents are used for both reconstruction and classification, encouraging the selected prototypes to capture input-relevant information across diverse datasets. To address the lack of standardized evaluation for prototype explanations, we assess three complementary aspects: perceptual similarity (LPIPS and DISTS) for perceived resemblance, subclass alignment, measuring whether prototypes match the input's fine-grained category beyond supervised labels, and information preservation (NMI), measuring how much input information is captured by selected prototypes. Experiments show that EMN improves visual similarity by 17.06%, subclass alignment by 104.57%, and NMI by 42.42% on average across CIFAR-10/100, MNIST, HAM10000, and CUB-200, while achieving the highest average classification accuracy (87.62%) among all methods. These findings underscore the effectiveness of neuroscience-inspired explanation mechanisms in advancing the interpretability and reliability of prototype-based models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Experiments show that EMN improves visual similarity by 17.06%, subclass alignment by 104.57%, and NMI by 42.42% on average across CIFAR-10/100, MNIST, HAM10000, and CUB-200, while achieving the highest average classification accuracy (87.62%) among all methods. These findings underscore the effectiveness of neuroscience-inspired explanation mechanisms in advancing the interpretability and reliability of prototype-based models.

</details>

## 61. Bridging Digital Deception and Physical Fidelity: A Spatio-Temporal Attack on Cognitive Radars

**中文标题**: Bridging Digital Deception and Physical Fidelity: A Spatio-Temporal Attack on Cognitive Radars

**作者**: Tongrun Xing, Yongjun Dai, Xuan Liao, Zhiwen Zhang, Tong Zhou et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27585v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27585v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27585v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As cognitive radars evolve towards the end-to-end processing of one-dimensional complex baseband sequences, deep networks face severe adversarial threats. However, existing attacks encounter dual limitations in practical electronic warfare: low cross-architecture transferability and severe vulnerability to physical channel distortions, which degrades their tactical reliability. To overcome these bottlenecks, we propose a physical prior-driven Segmented Spatio-Temporal Attack (SSTA). First, a global-local feature decoupling segmentation mechanism interrupts the adversarial gradients' dependence on long-range sequence coherence, bridging the cross-architecture feature gap. Second, moving beyond static norm constraints devoid of physical semantics, we abstract channel distortions into differentiable spatio-temporal operators, integrating them into momentum iterations via an Expectation over Transformation (EoT) strategy. Comprehensive cross-evaluations across 9 heterogeneous models spanning CNNs, RNNs, and Transformers demonstrate that SSTA outperforms the state-of-the-art baseline by 10.36% in Average Black-Box Attack Success Rate. Furthermore, baseband equivalent simulations confirm that the generated waveforms survive extreme physical distortions and, after matched filtering, achieve zero main-lobe attenuation (0 dB) with noise floor lift strictly within Constant False Alarm Rate tolerances. SSTA successfully unifies digital-domain deception and physical-domain fidelity, providing crucial support for CEW security assessment.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

To overcome these bottlenecks, we propose a physical prior-driven Segmented Spatio-Temporal Attack (SSTA). Furthermore, baseband equivalent simulations confirm that the generated waveforms survive extreme physical distortions and, after matched filtering, achieve zero main-lobe attenuation (0 dB) with noise floor lift strictly within Constant False Alarm Rate tolerances. SSTA successfully unifies digital-domain deception and physical-domain fidelity, providing crucial support for CEW security assessment.

</details>

## 62. Timing-Aware and Interpretable Self-Attention for Event-Based Wearable Task Transition Detection

**中文标题**: Timing-Aware and Interpretable Self-Attention for Event-Based Wearable Task Transition Detection

**作者**: Anda Ouyang, Siyuan Chen, Julien Epps

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27549v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27549v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27549v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Task transition detection aims to identify when a person shifts from one task to another, providing a foundation for continuous health monitoring and timely context-aware interventions. While statistical and deep feature representations have demonstrated strong classification performance, they often rely on aggregated or latent features that are difficult to interpret in behavioural terms. Event-based features provide a compact and interpretable alternative by encoding behavioural and physiological patterns as discrete tokens with explicit semantic meaning, making them well suited for behavioural analysis in health informatics. Self-attention offers a mechanism for modelling interactions among such tokens; however, conventional self-attention classifiers primarily rely on token order and do not explicitly incorporate timing or duration information. As a result, informative temporal cues in event representations may not be fully exploited for task transition detection. This study proposes an event-based time-aware self-attention framework that represents behavioural signals as sequences of discrete events augmented with onset timestamps and durations. Event timing is encoded using a parameter-efficient kernel-based representation, while a novel type-aware duration embedding modulates information propagation according to event persistence. Experiments on a semi-naturalistic wearable dataset show that the proposed model outperforms existing event-based approaches, achieves performance beyond or comparable with statistical and deep-learning models with a more compact input representation, and provides improved interpretability through integrated-gradient-based analysis. These results indicate that explicit modelling of event timing and duration enhances both classification performance and interpretability, supporting reliable deployment in biomedical and health informatics applications.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

While statistical and deep feature representations have demonstrated strong classification performance, they often rely on aggregated or latent features that are difficult to interpret in behavioural terms. </details>

## 63. BEACON: Bridging Multimodal Semantics and Asynchronous Propagation for Cloud System Anomaly Detection

**中文标题**: BEACON: Bridging Multimodal Semantics and Asynchronous Propagation for Cloud System Anomaly Detection

**作者**: Zhe Li, Tengxue Zhang, Yang Shu, Tung Kieu, Lunting Fan et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27542v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27542v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27542v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Modern large-scale cloud systems continuously generate massive and multimodal observability data, such as time-series metrics, log records, and system dependency graphs, which reflect the health state of the system. To ensure reliable cloud system operation, anomaly detection models identify abnormal states by analyzing these multimodal observations. However, existing approaches rely on simple fusion strategies and graph learning, ignoring the semantic gap among modalities and the asynchronous spatio-temporal dependencies across system instances over time. To this end, we propose BEACON, a framework to Bridge multimodal sEmantics and Asynchronous propagation for Cloud system anOmaly detectioN. Specifically, we introduce a Dynamic Interpretable Multimodal Aggregator (DIMA) that leverages an information-bottleneck mechanism to compress information from the semantics and temporal dynamics of metrics, and aligns them with logs in a shared prototype space. Furthermore, we propose an Asynchronous Spatio-Temporal Propagator (ASTP) that captures temporal dependencies with multi-scale temporal attention and models spatial relations with topology-aware aggregation. This enables asynchronous propagation of anomalies across system instances. Extensive experiments on three real-world cloud system datasets demonstrate that \textbf{BEACON} consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in anomaly detection.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Modern large-scale cloud systems continuously generate massive and multimodal observability data, such as time-series metrics, log records, and system dependency graphs, which reflect the health state of the system. To ensure reliable cloud system operation, anomaly detection models identify abnormal states by analyzing these multimodal observations. However, existing approaches rely on simple fusion strategies and graph learning, ignoring the semantic gap among modalities and the asynchronous spatio-temporal dependencies across system instances over time. To this end, we propose BEACON, a framework to Bridge multimodal sEmantics and Asynchronous propagation for Cloud system anOmaly detectioN. Specifically, we introduce a Dynamic Interpretable Multimodal Aggregator (DIMA) that leverages an information-bottleneck mechanism to compress information from the semantics and temporal dynamics of metrics, and aligns them with logs in a shared prototype space. Furthermore, we propose an Asynchronous Spatio-Temporal Propagator (ASTP) that captures temporal dependencies with multi-scale temporal attention and models spatial relations with topology-aware aggregation. This enables asynchronous propagation of anomalies across system instances. Extensive experiments on three real-world cloud system datasets demonstrate that \textbf{BEACON} consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in anomaly detection.

</details>

## 64. PRIMFLOW: Learning Trajectory Primitives for Few-Step Distillation

**中文标题**: PRIMFLOW: Learning Trajectory Primitives for Few-Step Distillation

**作者**: Hongyi Fang, Benjia Zhou

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27146v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27146v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27146v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Few-step trajectory distillation aims to learn accurate global updates from dense local supervision along a teacher's sampling trajectory. Existing approaches construct within-step dynamics using specific trajectory parameterizations or discrete interval predictions, tying the learned update to a prescribed temporal structure. We propose \method, which directly learns a continuous trajectory primitive representing cumulative displacement along the teacher trajectory. By supervising finite differences of the primitive with local teacher velocities, \method learns the accumulated motion directly, while obtaining global updates through endpoint differences. This formulation avoids prescribing a temporal basis or fixed query grid and instead learns the temporal evolution of cumulative displacement from local supervision. We further establish a local-to-global error bound to characterize how local regression errors affect full-interval displacement accuracy, and introduce endpoint absorption to eliminate the auxiliary primitive branch while preserving one-network-evaluation inference. Experiments on large-scale text-to-image generation show that \method achieves a T-FID of 20.92 and an HPSv2 score of 28.35 at one NFE, improving both metrics over a trajectory-distillation baseline. After endpoint absorption, it retains comparable performance, achieving a T-FID of 20.86 and an HPSv2 score of 28.33.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Few-step trajectory distillation aims to learn accurate global updates from dense local supervision along a teacher's sampling trajectory. Existing approaches construct within-step dynamics using specific trajectory parameterizations or discrete interval predictions, tying the learned update to a prescribed temporal structure. We propose \method, which directly learns a continuous trajectory primitive representing cumulative displacement along the teacher trajectory. By supervising finite differences of the primitive with local teacher velocities, \method learns the accumulated motion directly, while obtaining global updates through endpoint differences. This formulation avoids prescribing a temporal basis or fixed query grid and instead learns the temporal evolution of cumulative displacement from local supervision. We further establish a local-to-global error bound to characterize how local regression errors affect full-interval displacement accuracy, and introduce endpoint absorption to eliminate the auxiliary primitive branch while preserving one-network-evaluation inference. Experiments on large-scale text-to-image generation show that \method achieves a T-FID of 20.92 and an HPSv2 score of 28.35 at one NFE, improving both metrics over a trajectory-distillation baseline. After endpoint absorption, it retains comparable performance, achieving a T-FID of 20.86 and an HPSv2 score of 28.33.

</details>

## 65. LVMem: Image-Augmented Memory for Long-Horizon Question Answering

**中文标题**: LVMem: Image-Augmented Memory for Long-Horizon Question Answering

**作者**: Victoria Khoruzhaya, Maksim Kalmykov, Tuong Do Minh, Tatiana Zemskova, Yixin Zhu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27104v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27104v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27104v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reliable language-guided perception is essential for embodied agents, where questions about objects, events, and their locations must be answered from long observation histories. Unlike conventional visual question answering, realistic robotic settings require reasoning over extended temporal horizons and persistent scene memory. We build upon the view-based memory paradigm and propose an efficient framework for long-horizon spatio-temporal question answering. While existing view-based systems typically store either text captions or visual embeddings, we extend the ReMEmbR-style memory with image paths that link each stored description to its original visual content. To make the stored annotations more informative, we replace uniform sampling during memory construction with an optical-flow-based frame selection strategy (PSFR) and store an object-centric descriptive scene representation instead of plain captions. We further equip a VLM-based agent with an adaptive retrieval tool that returns the image associated with the most relevant caption, allowing the agent to decide when visual inspection is needed. On the OC-NaVQA benchmark, LVMem outperforms ReMEmbR on binary and positional metrics with both backbones and matches or exceeds a 4D scene graph DAAAM baseline on binary accuracy, while operating on a lightweight view-based memory that requires no explicit graph construction.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Unlike conventional visual question answering, realistic robotic settings require reasoning over extended temporal horizons and persistent scene memory. While existing view-based systems typically store either text captions or visual embeddings, we extend the ReMEmbR-style memory with image paths that link each stored description to its original visual content. To make the stored annotations more informative, we replace uniform sampling during memory construction with an optical-flow-based frame selection strategy (PSFR) and store an object-centric descriptive scene representation instead of plain captions. We further equip a VLM-based agent with an adaptive retrieval tool that returns the image associated with the most relevant caption, allowing the agent to decide when visual inspection is needed. On the OC-NaVQA benchmark, LVMem outperforms ReMEmbR on binary and positional metrics with both backbones and matches or exceeds a 4D scene graph DAAAM baseline on binary accuracy, while operating on a lightweight view-based memory that requires no explicit graph construction.

</details>

## 66. VideoMaMa++: Temporally Consistent Video Matting via Preserve-and-Refine Token

**中文标题**: VideoMaMa++: Temporally Consistent Video Matting via Preserve-and-Refine Token

**作者**: Sangbeom Lim, Seoung Wug Oh, Heeji Yoon, Seungryong Kim, Joon-Young Lee

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26857v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26857v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26857v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Diffusion-based video matting has recently emerged as a leading approach, leveraging video diffusion priors from internet-scale pretraining to achieve strong zero-shot generalization from limited synthetic data. However, the heavy compute of these diffusion backbones limits inference to a short chunk of just 6 to 24 frames, so even ordinary videos span multiple chunks. Because each chunk is matted independently from coarse mask guidance, existing methods fail to maintain consistency across chunk boundaries, producing flickering edges and discontinuous transparency. We address this with a mask-guided diffusion-matting framework built on two ideas. The RGB and mask conditioning are represented as separate token streams that interact through full spatio-temporal self-attention, letting the model adaptively combine fine RGB texture with coarse mask layout rather than committing to a fixed channel-wise fusion. On top of this, we propose a novel pair of learnable embeddings, the preserve token and the refine token, that act as a per-frame conditioning interface and enable a sliding-window inference scheme in which already-generated mattes are propagated across chunk boundaries for local temporal consistency, complemented by a globally shared reference anchor at every chunk to prevent error accumulation in long sequences without any additional training. Across standard video matting benchmarks and in-the-wild long footage, our framework outperforms prior diffusion-based methods in both fine-detail accuracy and temporal consistency, and uniquely maintains consistency across chunk boundaries on long videos.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Diffusion-based video matting has recently emerged as a leading approach, leveraging video diffusion priors from internet-scale pretraining to achieve strong zero-shot generalization from limited synthetic data. However, the heavy compute of these diffusion backbones limits inference to a short chunk of just 6 to 24 frames, so even ordinary videos span multiple chunks. Because each chunk is matted independently from coarse mask guidance, existing methods fail to maintain consistency across chunk boundaries, producing flickering edges and discontinuous transparency. We address this with a mask-guided diffusion-matting framework built on two ideas. The RGB and mask conditioning are represented as separate token streams that interact through full spatio-temporal self-attention, letting the model adaptively combine fine RGB texture with coarse mask layout rather than committing to a fixed channel-wise fusion. On top of this, we propose a novel pair of learnable embeddings, the preserve token and the refine token, that act as a per-frame conditioning interface and enable a sliding-window inference scheme in which already-generated mattes are propagated across chunk boundaries for local temporal consistency, complemented by a globally shared reference anchor at every chunk to prevent error accumulation in long sequences without any additional training. Across standard video matting benchmarks and in-the-wild long footage, our framework outperforms prior diffusion-based methods in both fine-detail accuracy and temporal consistency, and uniquely maintains consistency across chunk boundaries on long videos.

</details>

## 67. VitronEdit: Universal Visual Editing across Images, Videos, and 3D Scenes

**中文标题**: VitronEdit: Universal Visual Editing across Images, Videos, and 3D Scenes

**作者**: Jing Wang, Yanlin Li, Mong-Li Lee, Wynne Hsu, Shengqiong Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27049v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27049v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27049v1)

**项目主页**: https://vitron-llm.github.io/VitronEdit/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent advances in unified visual editing have begun to consolidate image and video editing within a single model, while 3D scene editing remains largely separate and constrained by scarce paired training data. Given the rich image and video editing supervision now available and the common editing semantics shared across modalities, a natural question is whether this knowledge can be transferred to data-scarce 3D editing. We present VitronEdit, a universal framework for image, video, and 3D scene editing. VitronEdit adopts a shared sequence representation and progressive multimodal training to transfer editing knowledge across modalities. We further introduce geometry-aware post-training, which leverages a pretrained geometry reconstruction model to provide depth and camera-pose supervision. Surprisingly, applying geometric supervision only to 3D samples yields the best overall performance, while image and video editing also improve despite receiving no direct geometric supervision, suggesting that geometric regularization transfers through the shared backbone. Our analysis further shows that image and video training mainly transfers editing semantics to 3D, whereas 3D supervision primarily improves multi-view consistency. The project page: https://vitron-llm.github.io/VitronEdit/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent advances in unified visual editing have begun to consolidate image and video editing within a single model, while 3D scene editing remains largely separate and constrained by scarce paired training data. Given the rich image and video editing supervision now available and the common editing semantics shared across modalities, a natural question is whether this knowledge can be transferred to data-scarce 3D editing. We present VitronEdit, a universal framework for image, video, and 3D scene editing. VitronEdit adopts a shared sequence representation and progressive multimodal training to transfer editing knowledge across modalities. We further introduce geometry-aware post-training, which leverages a pretrained geometry reconstruction model to provide depth and camera-pose supervision. Surprisingly, applying geometric supervision only to 3D samples yields the best overall performance, while image and video editing also improve despite receiving no direct geometric supervision, suggesting that geometric regularization transfers through the shared backbone. Our analysis further shows that image and video training mainly transfers editing semantics to 3D, whereas 3D supervision primarily improves multi-view consistency. The project page: https://vitron-llm.github.io/VitronEdit/.

</details>

---

## 68. Manga2Animate: An Agentic Framework for Faithful Manga Animation with Script and Storyboard Planning

**中文标题**: Manga2Animate: An Agentic Framework for Faithful Manga Animation with Script and Storyboard Planning

**作者**: Sheng-Wei Zhang, Hsi-An Chen, Yan-Ting Liou, Chih-Hsuan Chen, Shih-Chen Tseng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26851v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26851v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26851v1)

**项目主页**: https://annchenn.github.io/Manga2Animate/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Adapting existing manga into coherent animation that stays faithful to the source is a valuable yet challenging task. Existing methods either access the manga mainly through text, losing its characters and art style, or feed manga pages directly into video generation models, which misinterpret the manga's structure and produce incorrect storylines. In this paper, we propose Manga2Animate, a multi-agent framework that grounds every stage of generation in the source manga. We decompose the task into two intermediate representations, an animation script and a visual storyboard. Each representation is produced by a creator agent and reviewed against the source panels by a paired reviewer agent. We tailor representation-specific selection and dual-level feedback to progressively refine both before synthesizing the final animation. Extensive experiments and a user study demonstrate that Manga2Animate produces animations more faithful to the source manga and more consistent across shots than state-of-the-art approaches. Project page: https://annchenn.github.io/Manga2Animate/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Adapting existing manga into coherent animation that stays faithful to the source is a valuable yet challenging task. Existing methods either access the manga mainly through text, losing its characters and art style, or feed manga pages directly into video generation models, which misinterpret the manga's structure and produce incorrect storylines. In this paper, we propose Manga2Animate, a multi-agent framework that grounds every stage of generation in the source manga. We decompose the task into two intermediate representations, an animation script and a visual storyboard. Each representation is produced by a creator agent and reviewed against the source panels by a paired reviewer agent. We tailor representation-specific selection and dual-level feedback to progressively refine both before synthesizing the final animation. Extensive experiments and a user study demonstrate that Manga2Animate produces animations more faithful to the source manga and more consistent across shots than state-of-the-art approaches. Project page: https://annchenn.github.io/Manga2Animate/

</details>

## 69. SpecKCast: Spectral Kalman Forecasting for Diffusion Acceleration

**中文标题**: SpecKCast: Spectral Kalman Forecasting for Diffusion Acceleration

**作者**: Javad Rajabi, Hakki Can Karaimer, David B. Lindell, Babak Taati, Michael S. Brown

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26837v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26837v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26837v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) achieve high-fidelity image and video generation, but they suffer from substantial inference costs due to repeated transformer evaluations across many denoising steps. Feature caching reduces this cost by reusing features across denoising steps. Recent forecasting methods extend this idea by extrapolating features to skipped steps instead of reusing them. However, existing forecasting methods extrapolate the features they predict uniformly, even though the spatial-frequency components of these features change most at different stages of denoising. They also overlook that denoising progresses nonuniformly, so equal step intervals can correspond to very different amounts of change. These mismatches amplify forecast errors as more steps are skipped, limiting the achievable acceleration. We propose SpecKCast, a training-free method that forecasts the model output in the frequency domain. SpecKCast applies a 2D discrete cosine transform (DCT) to the output and tracks each coefficient with a lightweight Kalman filter along a band-specific time coordinate derived from the noise schedule. The filter tracks each coefficient's value and rate of change, and the transformer is evaluated only when the filter's forecast uncertainty exceeds a threshold. Experiments on FLUX.1-dev, Qwen-Image, Wan2.1-14B, and HunyuanVideo show that SpecKCast achieves a better trade-off between acceleration and fidelity than existing caching and forecasting methods, reaching up to 6x speedup with less than 0.05 GiB of additional memory.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) achieve high-fidelity image and video generation, but they suffer from substantial inference costs due to repeated transformer evaluations across many denoising steps. Feature caching reduces this cost by reusing features across denoising steps. Recent forecasting methods extend this idea by extrapolating features to skipped steps instead of reusing them. However, existing forecasting methods extrapolate the features they predict uniformly, even though the spatial-frequency components of these features change most at different stages of denoising. They also overlook that denoising progresses nonuniformly, so equal step intervals can correspond to very different amounts of change. These mismatches amplify forecast errors as more steps are skipped, limiting the achievable acceleration. We propose SpecKCast, a training-free method that forecasts the model output in the frequency domain. SpecKCast applies a 2D discrete cosine transform (DCT) to the output and tracks each coefficient with a lightweight Kalman filter along a band-specific time coordinate derived from the noise schedule. The filter tracks each coefficient's value and rate of change, and the transformer is evaluated only when the filter's forecast uncertainty exceeds a threshold. Experiments on FLUX.1-dev, Qwen-Image, Wan2.1-14B, and HunyuanVideo show that SpecKCast achieves a better trade-off between acceleration and fidelity than existing caching and forecasting methods, reaching up to 6x speedup with less than 0.05 GiB of additional memory.

</details>

## 70. Real-Time Radar Semantic and Panoptic Scene Understanding

**中文标题**: Real-Time Radar Semantic and Panoptic Scene Understanding

**作者**: Santosh Parit, Julia Hindel, Rohit Mohan, Georgi Pramatarov, Paul Newman et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26816v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26816v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26816v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robust scene understanding under varying environmental conditions is essential for safe autonomous driving. While cameras and LiDAR degrade in adverse weather and poor lighting, Frequency-Modulated Continuous-Wave (FMCW) spinning radar provides dependable 360° perception at extended ranges with minimal compute cost. However, real-time FMCW radar scene understanding is challenging due to complex signal returns, limited temporal resolution, and scarce annotated datasets. We introduce StreamRPS, a novel framework for streaming FMCW radar panoptic segmentation, comprising an automated label-generation pipeline via LiDAR scene completion and a real-time streaming network for semantic and panoptic segmentation. Our encoder-decoder streaming architecture comprises a radar-specific hierarchical Transformer encoder and task-specific decoders for semantic or panoptic prediction. Our encoder introduces a stem to capture range-dependent signatures, anisotropic patch embeddings to preserve angular resolution, and axial polar attention to separately model the periodic azimuth and bounded range axes. We further introduce azimuth roll rotation augmentation to simulate ego-vehicle rotation during training. Comprehensive experiments on the SAX and BOREAS datasets validate both our auto-labeling methodology and streaming segmentation network against established baselines. We outperform the strongest baseline by at least 1.4pp while processing sectors at over 400 FPS in a 4-sector streaming setting. We additionally provide manual annotations for 100 SAX radar scans, enabling independent evaluation of the entire framework.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robust scene understanding under varying environmental conditions is essential for safe autonomous driving. While cameras and LiDAR degrade in adverse weather and poor lighting, Frequency-Modulated Continuous-Wave (FMCW) spinning radar provides dependable 360° perception at extended ranges with minimal compute cost. However, real-time FMCW radar scene understanding is challenging due to complex signal returns, limited temporal resolution, and scarce annotated datasets. We introduce StreamRPS, a novel framework for streaming FMCW radar panoptic segmentation, comprising an automated label-generation pipeline via LiDAR scene completion and a real-time streaming network for semantic and panoptic segmentation. Our encoder-decoder streaming architecture comprises a radar-specific hierarchical Transformer encoder and task-specific decoders for semantic or panoptic prediction. Our encoder introduces a stem to capture range-dependent signatures, anisotropic patch embeddings to preserve angular resolution, and axial polar attention to separately model the periodic azimuth and bounded range axes. We further introduce azimuth roll rotation augmentation to simulate ego-vehicle rotation during training. Comprehensive experiments on the SAX and BOREAS datasets validate both our auto-labeling methodology and streaming segmentation network against established baselines. We outperform the strongest baseline by at least 1.4pp while processing sectors at over 400 FPS in a 4-sector streaming setting. We additionally provide manual annotations for 100 SAX radar scans, enabling independent evaluation of the entire framework.

</details>

## 71. PICA-WAM: Visual Perturbation Invariance via Cross-Appearance Self-Alignment in World Action Models

**中文标题**: PICA-WAM: Visual Perturbation Invariance via Cross-Appearance Self-Alignment in World Action Models

**作者**: Yude Zou, Yifei Yao, Guanbo Jiao, Jianming Ma, Guanjie Zheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26813v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26813v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26813v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/YudeZou/PICA-WAM-release

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) jointly predict future video and generate actions, yet remain sensitive to task-irrelevant visual perturbations, i.e., changes in visual appearance that should not affect robot behavior. We identify a gap in conventional WAM training: video prediction encourages representations to preserve appearance information, while action generation does not explicitly enforce invariance to task-irrelevant appearance. Data augmentation exposes WAMs to such perturbations but does not require perturbed and nominal observations to produce consistent action representations, and alignment with external pretrained features does not guarantee the desired invariance. To address this gap, we propose PICA-WAM, a self-supervised training framework that casts perturbation invariance as an auxiliary cross-appearance self-alignment task for WAMs. An exponential-moving-average (EMA) teacher processes the nominal observation to provide a deeper-layer target, while the online WAM processes a perturbed observation and aligns its shallower-layer action representation to this target through a lightweight projection head. Across four strong WAMs evaluated on RoboTwin 2.0, LIBERO-Plus, EBench, and a real robot, PICA-WAM consistently improves robustness under visual perturbations, preserves nominal performance in simulation, and raises the real robot success rate. PICA-WAM further reduces the sensitivity of video features to task-irrelevant appearance and its propagation into actions, as shown by paired layer-wise diagnostics. Code: https://github.com/YudeZou/PICA-WAM-release

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) jointly predict future video and generate actions, yet remain sensitive to task-irrelevant visual perturbations, i.e., changes in visual appearance that should not affect robot behavior. We identify a gap in conventional WAM training: video prediction encourages representations to preserve appearance information, while action generation does not explicitly enforce invariance to task-irrelevant appearance. Data augmentation exposes WAMs to such perturbations but does not require perturbed and nominal observations to produce consistent action representations, and alignment with external pretrained features does not guarantee the desired invariance. To address this gap, we propose PICA-WAM, a self-supervised training framework that casts perturbation invariance as an auxiliary cross-appearance self-alignment task for WAMs. An exponential-moving-average (EMA) teacher processes the nominal observation to provide a deeper-layer target, while the online WAM processes a perturbed observation and aligns its shallower-layer action representation to this target through a lightweight projection head. Across four strong WAMs evaluated on RoboTwin 2.0, LIBERO-Plus, EBench, and a real robot, PICA-WAM consistently improves robustness under visual perturbations, preserves nominal performance in simulation, and raises the real robot success rate. PICA-WAM further reduces the sensitivity of video features to task-irrelevant appearance and its propagation into actions, as shown by paired layer-wise diagnostics. Code: https://github.com/YudeZou/PICA-WAM-release

</details>
