## 72. State World Models: Execute the Rules, Generate the Pixels

**中文标题**: State World Models: Execute the Rules, Generate the Pixels

**作者**: Chan Yan Ki, Yanran Zhang, Wenzhao Zheng, Xiaofeng Wang, Zheng Zhu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26811v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26811v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26811v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Existing world models can generate action-conditioned video from a single image. Many focus on camera movement, allowing users to explore the generated scene. However, navigation alone does not ensure that an action follows persistent rules when its outcome depends on state that is invisible in the current frame. We present Stateful Harness, a framework that separates world evolution from video rendering to support stateful and rule-governed interaction. It maintains an evolving world state under specified rules, renders each resulting event with a frozen video model, and uses the generated clip to update the world state for the next turn. At each turn, the harness resolves the action selected by the user and compiles the resulting event into an instruction, while a preview clip from an editable 3D scene provides spatial and camera guidance. To evaluate rule compliance, we build WorldState-Bench, a benchmark of 50 counterfactual twin pairs that share an initial frame and action but differ in hidden state and required outcome. Under rule-only prompting, the best of the 11 evaluated video backends produces the required endings for both cases in 32% of pairs. For MiniMax-H3, specifying the required endings raises this rate from 32% to 64%, while the full harness reaches 92% with the same frozen model. These results support separating rule execution from video rendering for world interactions with hidden state.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing world models can generate action-conditioned video from a single image. Many focus on camera movement, allowing users to explore the generated scene. However, navigation alone does not ensure that an action follows persistent rules when its outcome depends on state that is invisible in the current frame. We present Stateful Harness, a framework that separates world evolution from video rendering to support stateful and rule-governed interaction. It maintains an evolving world state under specified rules, renders each resulting event with a frozen video model, and uses the generated clip to update the world state for the next turn. At each turn, the harness resolves the action selected by the user and compiles the resulting event into an instruction, while a preview clip from an editable 3D scene provides spatial and camera guidance. To evaluate rule compliance, we build WorldState-Bench, a benchmark of 50 counterfactual twin pairs that share an initial frame and action but differ in hidden state and required outcome. Under rule-only prompting, the best of the 11 evaluated video backends produces the required endings for both cases in 32% of pairs. For MiniMax-H3, specifying the required endings raises this rate from 32% to 64%, while the full harness reaches 92% with the same frozen model. These results support separating rule execution from video rendering for world interactions with hidden state.

</details>

## 73. Mise-en-Video: Fine-Grained 3D Control for Multi-Shot Video Generation

**中文标题**: Mise-en-Video: Fine-Grained 3D Control for Multi-Shot Video Generation

**作者**: Yingshuang Zou, Xiaoyu Shi, Yawen Luo, Ziang Li, Qinghe Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26801v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26801v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26801v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent advances in text-to-video generation have enabled high-fidelity single-shot synthesis, yet practical storytelling creation requires multi-shot videos with both strong inter-shot consistency and fine-grained controllability. Existing multi-shot video generation approaches achieve strong consistency but still fall short in controllability. In this paper, we propose Mise-en-Video (MeV), a highly controllable multi-shot video generation framework that allows video creators to conduct staging the multi-shot in the 3D space and then generate the corresponding multi-shot video. To achieve this, our framework combines a hierarchical attention mechanism for multi-level narrative consistency with 3D-aware layout guidance for precise, per-shot spatial control. To support training and evaluation, we develop standardized annotation pipelines and construct a 160K-video dataset with hierarchical multi-shot texts and oriented 3D bounding boxes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MeV consistently outperforms strong baselines in video quality, inter-shot consistency, and subject-level controllability.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent advances in text-to-video generation have enabled high-fidelity single-shot synthesis, yet practical storytelling creation requires multi-shot videos with both strong inter-shot consistency and fine-grained controllability. Existing multi-shot video generation approaches achieve strong consistency but still fall short in controllability. In this paper, we propose Mise-en-Video (MeV), a highly controllable multi-shot video generation framework that allows video creators to conduct staging the multi-shot in the 3D space and then generate the corresponding multi-shot video. To achieve this, our framework combines a hierarchical attention mechanism for multi-level narrative consistency with 3D-aware layout guidance for precise, per-shot spatial control. To support training and evaluation, we develop standardized annotation pipelines and construct a 160K-video dataset with hierarchical multi-shot texts and oriented 3D bounding boxes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MeV consistently outperforms strong baselines in video quality, inter-shot consistency, and subject-level controllability.

</details>

## 74. V-JEPA Hand: Simple, Accurate and Fast 3D Hand and Camera Trajectory Recovery from Egocentric Videos

**中文标题**: V-JEPA Hand: Simple, Accurate and Fast 3D Hand and Camera Trajectory Recovery from Egocentric Videos

**作者**: Ruicheng Li, Qixiu Li, Yu Deng, Sicheng Xu, Jiaolong Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26780v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26780v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26780v1)

**项目主页**: https://qft-333.github.io/V-JEPA-Hand-Page/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Egocentric video supports diverse applications, including robot learning, augmented reality, and human activity understanding. Recovering metric 3D hand trajectories from such video requires estimating both hand motion and camera egomotion to express the hands in a consistent reference frame. Many existing pipelines recover hand and camera motion in separate stages, combining hand detection, hand reconstruction, camera tracking, and trajectory refinement. We present V-JEPA Hand, a simple feed-forward video model that jointly reconstructs bilateral hand motion and camera motion from egocentric clips. It uses a self-supervised V-JEPA 2.1 encoder to extract spatio-temporal features from full-frame video and a query-based predictor to decode these features into per-frame hand poses, hand presence, and camera motion. We use the predicted camera motion to express the hand trajectories in a common world frame. The model accepts known focal lengths or predicts them when unavailable. It reconstructs each clip in a single forward pass. We train both components end to end in a single stage on a mixture of diverse pseudo-labelled videos and high-precision 3D annotations. A single model achieves the lowest coverage-aware mean per-joint position error (MPJPE) among comparable baselines on ARCTIC, HOT3D, and EgoStandard. It also yields lower world-frame trajectory errors than the compared SLAM-based pipelines. Ablations support the benefits of video pretraining, temporal context, and mixed supervision. V-JEPA Hand achieves a forward-pass throughput of 93.4 FPS on a single RTX A6000. Project page: https://qft-333.github.io/V-JEPA-Hand-Page/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Egocentric video supports diverse applications, including robot learning, augmented reality, and human activity understanding. Recovering metric 3D hand trajectories from such video requires estimating both hand motion and camera egomotion to express the hands in a consistent reference frame. Many existing pipelines recover hand and camera motion in separate stages, combining hand detection, hand reconstruction, camera tracking, and trajectory refinement. We use the predicted camera motion to express the hand trajectories in a common world frame. The model accepts known focal lengths or predicts them when unavailable. It reconstructs each clip in a single forward pass. We train both components end to end in a single stage on a mixture of diverse pseudo-labelled videos and high-precision 3D annotations. A single model achieves the lowest coverage-aware mean per-joint position error (MPJPE) among comparable baselines on ARCTIC, HOT3D, and EgoStandard. It also yields lower world-frame trajectory errors than the compared SLAM-based pipelines. Ablations support the benefits of video pretraining, temporal context, and mixed supervision. V-JEPA Hand achieves a forward-pass throughput of 93.4 FPS on a single RTX A6000. Project page: https://qft-333.github.io/V-JEPA-Hand-Page/.

</details>

## 75. PVBench: Quantifying Physical Plausibility in Video Generation with Object-Level Physical Measurements

**中文标题**: PVBench: Quantifying Physical Plausibility in Video Generation with Object-Level Physical Measurements

**作者**: Kaiwei Chen, Moran Li, Zhenye Gan, Yabiao Wang, Chengjie Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26711v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26711v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26711v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent video generation models produce visually compelling outputs, yet their physical plausibility remains poorly measured. Physical plausibility asks whether generated objects obey geometry, kinematics, dynamics, and conservation laws. Existing benchmarks rely on coarse visual-language model judgments or target embodied world models rather than video generation itself. We introduce PVBench, a physical video benchmark that quantitatively measures the plausibility of generated videos across 46 dimensions organized into seven domains, spanning: i) instruction and semantic fidelity, ii) behavioral and causal consistency, iii) perceptual and temporal quality, iv) geometric and spatial consistency, v) material properties and field states, vi) kinematic and spatiotemporal persistence, vii) and mechanical plausibility with conservation laws. PVBench comprises 600 carefully crafted prompts, of which 30% involve physical embodied agents such as robot arms, humanoids, quadrupeds, and multi-agent systems. For challenging physically tractable dimensions, it pairs each with specialized vision models that derive verifiable, physically grounded measurements, such as object-level 6D pose, metric scale, surface normals, kinematics, and numerical conservation-law residuals. It thus avoids relying solely on subjective scoring, while supporting both text-to-video and image-to-video generation. Finally, we validate the agreement between automatic metrics and human judgments through human evaluation. Meanwhile, we evaluate mainstream open-source and closed-source models and present several insights. By releasing the benchmark, prompts, and calibration protocol, we aim to foster physically grounded video generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

PVBench comprises 600 carefully crafted prompts, of which 30% involve physical embodied agents such as robot arms, humanoids, quadrupeds, and multi-agent systems. It thus avoids relying solely on subjective scoring, while supporting both text-to-video and image-to-video generation. Finally, we validate the agreement between automatic metrics and human judgments through human evaluation. Meanwhile, we evaluate mainstream open-source and closed-source models and present several insights. By releasing the benchmark, prompts, and calibration protocol, we aim to foster physically grounded video generation.

</details>

## 76. From Short Clips to Real-Time Streams: Identity-Aware Supervision and Context-Efficient Generation for Video Face Swapping

**中文标题**: From Short Clips to Real-Time Streams: Identity-Aware Supervision and Context-Efficient Generation for Video Face Swapping

**作者**: Zekai Luo, Yuwei Liang, Ruiqi Ma, Haoyu Huang, Xuehao Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26681v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26681v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26681v1)

**项目主页**: https://aim-uofa.github.io/StreamingSwap/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video face swapping transfers the reference identity to a target video while preserving its other attributes and motion. Despite recent progress in reference-guided flow-based methods, jointly achieving faithful identity transfer, high fidelity, and real-time generation over long video streams remains challenging. To address this challenge, we rethink the task-specific structure of reference-guided video face swapping. First, face swapping changes only identity while preserving most visual content, yet flow-matching supervision is weakly sensitive to these changes. We therefore develop Joint Rec-ID Loss, which balances explicit identity supervision and latent reconstruction according to the noise level. Second, the target video already provides non-edited attributes and temporal dynamics, enabling generation with fewer spatial tokens and shorter temporal contexts through Face-RoI Generation and Short-Context Rollout Training. By integrating these designs, we propose StreamingSwap, a reference-guided flow-matching framework for real-time streaming video face swapping. We also introduce HumanFaceVid, comprising 1,000 hours of cinematic training data and evaluation sets of 200 short and 200 long videos. Experiments demonstrate that our method achieves competitive identity transfer and visual quality at 20 FPS on a single NVIDIA RTX 4090 GPU, with stable generation over videos up to 10 minutes long. Project webpage: https://aim-uofa.github.io/StreamingSwap/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video face swapping transfers the reference identity to a target video while preserving its other attributes and motion. Despite recent progress in reference-guided flow-based methods, jointly achieving faithful identity transfer, high fidelity, and real-time generation over long video streams remains challenging. To address this challenge, we rethink the task-specific structure of reference-guided video face swapping. We therefore develop Joint Rec-ID Loss, which balances explicit identity supervision and latent reconstruction according to the noise level. By integrating these designs, we propose StreamingSwap, a reference-guided flow-matching framework for real-time streaming video face swapping. We also introduce HumanFaceVid, comprising 1,000 hours of cinematic training data and evaluation sets of 200 short and 200 long videos. Experiments demonstrate that our method achieves competitive identity transfer and visual quality at 20 FPS on a single NVIDIA RTX 4090 GPU, with stable generation over videos up to 10 minutes long. Project webpage: https://aim-uofa.github.io/StreamingSwap/

</details>

## 77. Drive2Gauss: FeedForward 4D Gaussian Reconstruction from Multimodal Generative Driving Videos

**中文标题**: Drive2Gauss: FeedForward 4D Gaussian Reconstruction from Multimodal Generative Driving Videos

**作者**: Tong Zhao, Zhiyuan Han, Lin Chen, Bowen Xie, Yunxi Qiao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26585v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26585v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26585v1)

**项目主页**: https://zttt04.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Generating reusable driving environments requires both controllable future observations and an explicit representation that supports rendering from different viewpoints, yet existing methods still recover explicit scenes from generated RGB videos through optimization for each sequence. To overcome these limitations, we present Drive2Gauss, a feedforward framework that converts jointly generated RGB, metric depth, and dynamic flow into dynamic 4D Gaussian scenes. A Multimodal Video Generator predicts the three modalities from reference multiview images and future control signals. Furthermore, we introduce a Gaussian Query Decoder that uses depth to initialize sparse 3D queries and flow to associate the queries across time, and integrates appearance information through spatiotemporal feature aggregation and iterative query refinement. The reconstruction stage operates in a single feedforward pass without optimization for each scene. Extensive results on nuScenes demonstrate that our approach attains an FID of 6.38 and an FVD of 12.43 for future RGB generation, a PSNR of 29.91 dB when reconstructing its generated observations, and supports both direct rendering under lateral camera shifts and downstream video fixing. Code and more visualizations are available at https://zttt04. github.io/Drive2Gauss_page/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Generating reusable driving environments requires both controllable future observations and an explicit representation that supports rendering from different viewpoints, yet existing methods still recover explicit scenes from generated RGB videos through optimization for each sequence. To overcome these limitations, we present Drive2Gauss, a feedforward framework that converts jointly generated RGB, metric depth, and dynamic flow into dynamic 4D Gaussian scenes. A Multimodal Video Generator predicts the three modalities from reference multiview images and future control signals. Furthermore, we introduce a Gaussian Query Decoder that uses depth to initialize sparse 3D queries and flow to associate the queries across time, and integrates appearance information through spatiotemporal feature aggregation and iterative query refinement. The reconstruction stage operates in a single feedforward pass without optimization for each scene. Extensive results on nuScenes demonstrate that our approach attains an FID of 6.38 and an FVD of 12.43 for future RGB generation, a PSNR of 29.91 dB when reconstructing its generated observations, and supports both direct rendering under lateral camera shifts and downstream video fixing. Code and more visualizations are available at https://zttt04. github.io/Drive2Gauss_page/.

</details>

---

## 78. PrismWAM: Decoupling State Representations in World Action Models

**中文标题**: PrismWAM: Decoupling State Representations in World Action Models

**作者**: Tianyu Xin, Wanqi Zhong, Bowen Hu, Zhangchi Guo, Lijie Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26564v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26564v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26564v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) typically represent the world through latent embeddings from pretrained video models, which entangle heterogeneous aspects of the world, from how it looks (e.g., texture and color) to how it is physically configured (e.g., occupancy and pose). This raises an underexplored question: $\textbf{what should constitute the state of a WAM?}$ We argue that the world state should separate how the world appears from how the world evolves under action: the former captures semantic context, while the latter is governed by physical state. To this end, we propose $\textbf{PrismWAM}$, which decouples physics from semantics while jointly modeling state and action. PrismWAM factorizes an embodied task into a $\mathrm{Semantic\ Planning}\rightarrow\mathrm{Physical\ Rollout}\rightarrow\mathrm{Executable\ Action}$ pipeline: given only the initial RGB observation, semantic planning learns to predict the final goal image, while physical rollout predicts the fine-grained evolution toward this goal in depth space; executable actions are then generated from the depth rollout. Without any embodied action pretraining or additional data augmentation, PrismWAM achieves strong manipulation performance in both simulation and the real world. Notably, PrismWAM even surpasses a broad range of models with large-scale embodied action pretraining (e.g., OpenWAM-$α$). More broadly, we reframe World Action Models from modeling how the world looks to how it responds to action, paving the way for action-centric world models grounded in occupancy, flow, tactile sensing, proprioception, and beyond.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This raises an underexplored question: $\textbf{what should constitute the state of a WAM?}$ We argue that the world state should separate how the world appears from how the world evolves under action: the former captures semantic context, while the latter is governed by physical state. To this end, we propose $\textbf{PrismWAM}$, which decouples physics from semantics while jointly modeling state and action. PrismWAM factorizes an embodied task into a $\mathrm{Semantic\ Planning}\rightarrow\mathrm{Physical\ Rollout}\rightarrow\mathrm{Executable\ Action}$ pipeline: given only the initial RGB observation, semantic planning learns to predict the final goal image, while physical rollout predicts the fine-grained evolution toward this goal in depth space; executable actions are then generated from the depth rollout. Without any embodied action pretraining or additional data augmentation, PrismWAM achieves strong manipulation performance in both simulation and the real world. Notably, PrismWAM even surpasses a broad range of models with large-scale embodied action pretraining (e.g., OpenWAM-$α$). More broadly, we reframe World Action Models from modeling how the world looks to how it responds to action, paving the way for action-centric world models grounded in occupancy, flow, tactile sensing, proprioception, and beyond.

</details>

## 79. Holi-World: Building Holistic World Intelligence from Human-Ego Experience

**中文标题**: Holi-World: Building Holistic World Intelligence from Human-Ego Experience

**作者**: Hao Li, Xiyuan Zheng, Yuanyuan Gao, Yalun Dai, Xinhao Ji et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26549v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26549v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26549v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Human egocentric experience offers a rich foundation for learning how the world is organized and how it changes through interaction. Realizing this potential at scale requires turning growing volumes of recordings into learning resources that connect scene structure, object semantics, and interaction histories across viewpoints and time, while reducing dependence on costly manual annotation. We introduce HOLI-WORLD, an integrated ecosystem connecting experience capture, world representation, automated data construction, model learning, and independent evaluation. To sup- port learning at scale, HOLIWORLD-Foundry combines complementary static and dynamic perception to automatically construct world records that connect persistent scene structure and evolving object states with interaction histories and supporting observations. HOLIWORLD-Data organizes these records from approximately 100 self-collected scenes into grounded learning resources, including approximately 80K QA pairs used to train HOLIWORLD-VL-4B. Independent human annotations of heldout recordings form HOLIWORLD-Bench, supporting evaluation of both annotation quality and learned spatial and temporal understanding. Experiments on ScanNet, ScanNet++, Aria Digital Twin, and HOLIWORLD-Bench demonstrate strong performance in static perception and dynamic tracking, while training on HOLIWORLD-Data substantially improves HOLIWORLD-VL-4B's spatial and temporal reasoning on HOLI- WORLD-Bench and EgoDynamic4D. HOLIWORLD provides a practical foundation for expanding learning resources from the continuous flow of human experience and advancing machine understanding of the dynamic world.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Human egocentric experience offers a rich foundation for learning how the world is organized and how it changes through interaction. Realizing this potential at scale requires turning growing volumes of recordings into learning resources that connect scene structure, object semantics, and interaction histories across viewpoints and time, while reducing dependence on costly manual annotation. We introduce HOLI-WORLD, an integrated ecosystem connecting experience capture, world representation, automated data construction, model learning, and independent evaluation. To sup- port learning at scale, HOLIWORLD-Foundry combines complementary static and dynamic perception to automatically construct world records that connect persistent scene structure and evolving object states with interaction histories and supporting observations. HOLIWORLD-Data organizes these records from approximately 100 self-collected scenes into grounded learning resources, including approximately 80K QA pairs used to train HOLIWORLD-VL-4B. Independent human annotations of heldout recordings form HOLIWORLD-Bench, supporting evaluation of both annotation quality and learned spatial and temporal understanding. Experiments on ScanNet, ScanNet++, Aria Digital Twin, and HOLIWORLD-Bench demonstrate strong performance in static perception and dynamic tracking, while training on HOLIWORLD-Data substantially improves HOLIWORLD-VL-4B's spatial and temporal reasoning on HOLI- WORLD-Bench and EgoDynamic4D. HOLIWORLD provides a practical foundation for expanding learning resources from the continuous flow of human experience and advancing machine understanding of the dynamic world.

</details>

## 80. Long-form Video-to-Text Summarization using Recursive Language Models

**中文标题**: Long-form Video-to-Text Summarization using Recursive Language Models

**作者**: Alessia Intini, Lorenzo Vaiani, Enrico Chen, Luca Cagliero

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26431v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26431v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26431v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

While VideoLLMs have made significant progress in understanding short video benchmarks, they often struggle to summarize long-form ones. This is mainly due to performance degradation caused by long-context prompts, the inherent difficulty of preserving temporal continuity and a lack of semantically rich concepts in event descriptions. To tackle these issues, this paper introduces the first recursive Video Language Summarization Model (RVLSM) designed for video processing and tailored to long-form video-to-text summarization. RVLSM recursively decomposes videos into semantically coherent segments and delegates chunk-level reasoning to VideoLLM sub-models and external tools. It employs (1) Semantic Temporal Decomposition (STD), which preserves temporal coherence and continuity through a scene-aware chunking strategy that dynamically partitions videos according to semantic boundaries, and (2) Adaptive Multimodal Tool Calling (AMTC), which ensures semantic richness of the summary via a routing mechanism that selectively invokes auxiliary modules such as action recognition, optical character recognition, automatic speech recognition, and object detection. Experiments on the VideoXum and MixTyVSum benchmarks show that RVLSM consistently improves over zero-shot VideoLLM baselines across multiple model settings and performance metrics, and is competitive with a state-of-the-art fine-tuned model for video-to-text summarization. Importantly, the named entities in the RVLSM's summaries align more closely with those detected in the ground-truth summaries than those detected by baseline and state-of-the-art methods, confirming the saliency of the event descriptions it generates.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

While VideoLLMs have made significant progress in understanding short video benchmarks, they often struggle to summarize long-form ones. This is mainly due to performance degradation caused by long-context prompts, the inherent difficulty of preserving temporal continuity and a lack of semantically rich concepts in event descriptions. To tackle these issues, this paper introduces the first recursive Video Language Summarization Model (RVLSM) designed for video processing and tailored to long-form video-to-text summarization. RVLSM recursively decomposes videos into semantically coherent segments and delegates chunk-level reasoning to VideoLLM sub-models and external tools. It employs (1) Semantic Temporal Decomposition (STD), which preserves temporal coherence and continuity through a scene-aware chunking strategy that dynamically partitions videos according to semantic boundaries, and (2) Adaptive Multimodal Tool Calling (AMTC), which ensures semantic richness of the summary via a routing mechanism that selectively invokes auxiliary modules such as action recognition, optical character recognition, automatic speech recognition, and object detection. Experiments on the VideoXum and MixTyVSum benchmarks show that RVLSM consistently improves over zero-shot VideoLLM baselines across multiple model settings and performance metrics, and is competitive with a state-of-the-art fine-tuned model for video-to-text summarization. Importantly, the named entities in the RVLSM's summaries align more closely with those detected in the ground-truth summaries than those detected by baseline and state-of-the-art methods, confirming the saliency of the event descriptions it generates.

</details>

## 81. From Legacy to Contemporary: Scalable Color Modernization for Long Videos

**中文标题**: From Legacy to Contemporary: Scalable Color Modernization for Long Videos

**作者**: Dongyun Kim, Jiwoo Choi, Jaeseong Lee, Seon Joo Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26391v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26391v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26391v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/jiwoomood/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Bringing legacy video back to contemporary screens does not necessarily make it look contemporary. Earlier imaging pipelines often leave color casts, muted chroma, weak con- trast, and tonal imbalance, while correcting these character- istics still relies heavily on expert colorists and scales poorly to long, multi-shot videos. We formulate long video color modernization, a task that updates the chromatic and tonal appearance of legacy color footage while preserving scene content and coherence across frames and shots. We introduce a scalable, training-free framework that jointly modernizes grids of temporally adjacent keyframes using a pretrained text-guided image editing model, producing coherent con- temporary references without paired supervision. Compact shot-specific color transforms then propagate these adjust- ments throughout each shot, preserving spatial structure and temporal stability. We conduct a multidimensional evaluation on diverse legacy videos spanning five decades. We combine a human evaluation of perceived modernness with quantita- tive measures of content fidelity, temporal stability, percep- tual quality, and efficiency, and introduce Source-Weighted Inter-shot Grade Consistency (SW-IGC) to assess grade co- herence across shots. On our evaluation set, our frame- work achieves the highest perceived modernness while re- maining competitive across complementary measures, open- ing a path toward the systematic study of long video color modernization. Code is available at https://github.com/jiwoomood/ Video_Color_Modernization.git

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We formulate long video color modernization, a task that updates the chromatic and tonal appearance of legacy color footage while preserving scene content and coherence across frames and shots. Compact shot-specific color transforms then propagate these adjust- ments throughout each shot, preserving spatial structure and temporal stability. We conduct a multidimensional evaluation on diverse legacy videos spanning five decades. We combine a human evaluation of perceived modernness with quantita- tive measures of content fidelity, temporal stability, percep- tual quality, and efficiency, and introduce Source-Weighted Inter-shot Grade Consistency (SW-IGC) to assess grade co- herence across shots. On our evaluation set, our frame- work achieves the highest perceived modernness while re- maining competitive across complementary measures, open- ing a path toward the systematic study of long video color modernization. Code is available at https://github.com/jiwoomood/ Video_Color_Modernization.git

</details>

## 82. TIM: Temporal Decoupling with Iterative Mutual-Refinement Model for Longitudinal Radiology Report Generation

**中文标题**: TIM: Temporal Decoupling with Iterative Mutual-Refinement Model for Longitudinal Radiology Report Generation

**作者**: Yiheng Dong, Yi Lin, Shilong Huang, Xiyan Yang, Xin Yang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26271v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26271v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26271v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/yihengd/TIM.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Automatic radiology report generation (RRG) aims to translate medical images into diagnostic text, reducing radiologists' workload and standardizing clinical documentation. Nonetheless, existing approaches mainly focus on single-timepoint analysis and fail to capture temporal disease evolution across longitudinal examinations. While recent longitudinal RRG (LRRG) approaches incorporate historical data, they often combine images from different time points within a single representation space, leading to blurred semantics and inconsistent temporal reasoning. In this work, we propose a Temporal Decoupling with Iterative Mutual-Refinement Model (TIM), a two-stage framework that explicitly decouples spatial pathology from temporal progression and iteratively refines reports through mutual feedback. Stage I performs temporal-decoupled representation learning, separating temporal evolution patterns from disease-specific features and generating radiology reports for both prior and current studies. Stage II introduces a mutual report refinement mechanism that identifies diagnostic inconsistencies within prior reports and iteratively rectifies both prior and current reports through error-sensitive feedback. Experiments on the Longitudinal-MIMIC dataset demonstrate that TIM surpasses existing single-image and longitudinal baselines, achieving new state-of-the-art performance across both language and clinical metrics. Code is available at https://github.com/yihengd/TIM.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Automatic radiology report generation (RRG) aims to translate medical images into diagnostic text, reducing radiologists' workload and standardizing clinical documentation. Nonetheless, existing approaches mainly focus on single-timepoint analysis and fail to capture temporal disease evolution across longitudinal examinations. While recent longitudinal RRG (LRRG) approaches incorporate historical data, they often combine images from different time points within a single representation space, leading to blurred semantics and inconsistent temporal reasoning. In this work, we propose a Temporal Decoupling with Iterative Mutual-Refinement Model (TIM), a two-stage framework that explicitly decouples spatial pathology from temporal progression and iteratively refines reports through mutual feedback. Stage I performs temporal-decoupled representation learning, separating temporal evolution patterns from disease-specific features and generating radiology reports for both prior and current studies. Stage II introduces a mutual report refinement mechanism that identifies diagnostic inconsistencies within prior reports and iteratively rectifies both prior and current reports through error-sensitive feedback. Experiments on the Longitudinal-MIMIC dataset demonstrate that TIM surpasses existing single-image and longitudinal baselines, achieving new state-of-the-art performance across both language and clinical metrics. Code is available at https://github.com/yihengd/TIM.

</details>

## 83. Dynamics-Aware Sparse Attention for Efficient Autoregressive Video Diffusion

**中文标题**: Dynamics-Aware Sparse Attention for Efficient Autoregressive Video Diffusion

**作者**: Yuanyu He, Zhuokun Chen, Yefei He, Zhiwei Tang, Jiasheng Tang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26262v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26262v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26262v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autoregressive Diffusion Transformers have emerged as a powerful paradigm for long video generation. However, they are often constrained by prohibitive computational overhead and substantial memory footprints. This inefficiency arises from dense computation mechanisms that allocate uniform processing resources across all spatiotemporal regions, failing to leverage the inherent temporal redundancy of video where motion is typically confined to sparse areas. In this paper, we propose Dynamics-Aware Sparse Attention (DASA), a unified framework that shifts from dense computation to sparse, motion-driven generation to eliminate both storage and computational redundancies. Inspired by video codec principles, our approach explicitly decouples video content into dynamic and static regions. To mitigate memory bottlenecks, we selectively compress the historical KV cache by retaining only dynamic features within the generated chunk. Furthermore, to reduce computational overhead, we employ a region-level computation allocation strategy based on localized dynamics. Additionally, a dynamics-guided distillation method is introduced to enhance performance via a lightweight distillation training process. Experiments on VBench demonstrate that our approach accelerates autoregressive video generation by 2.16x, while preserving high visual fidelity.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autoregressive Diffusion Transformers have emerged as a powerful paradigm for long video generation. However, they are often constrained by prohibitive computational overhead and substantial memory footprints. This inefficiency arises from dense computation mechanisms that allocate uniform processing resources across all spatiotemporal regions, failing to leverage the inherent temporal redundancy of video where motion is typically confined to sparse areas. In this paper, we propose Dynamics-Aware Sparse Attention (DASA), a unified framework that shifts from dense computation to sparse, motion-driven generation to eliminate both storage and computational redundancies. Inspired by video codec principles, our approach explicitly decouples video content into dynamic and static regions. To mitigate memory bottlenecks, we selectively compress the historical KV cache by retaining only dynamic features within the generated chunk. Furthermore, to reduce computational overhead, we employ a region-level computation allocation strategy based on localized dynamics. Additionally, a dynamics-guided distillation method is introduced to enhance performance via a lightweight distillation training process. Experiments on VBench demonstrate that our approach accelerates autoregressive video generation by 2.16x, while preserving high visual fidelity.

</details>

## 84. RECAP: Reciprocal Sparse Attention and Hierarchical Caching for Efficient Video Diffusion

**中文标题**: RECAP: Reciprocal Sparse Attention and Hierarchical Caching for Efficient Video Diffusion

**作者**: Tiange Xu, Kai Liu, Linghe Kong, Yulun Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26244v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26244v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26244v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos but remain computationally expensive. Sparse attention and temporal caching reduce computation within and across denoising steps, respectively, offering a natural composition. However, predictions produced with sparse attention become sources for temporal reuse, coupling their errors along the sampling trajectory. We observe that improving the accuracy of these sources recovers fidelity without changing the reuse schedule, and that sparsification errors remain directionally aligned across nearby steps, suggesting that correction information can be shared rather than recomputed. These observations motivate RECAP, a training-free method that couples sparse attention with hierarchical caching through reciprocal correction, adaptively coordinated by online error feedback. Cached dense refinement corrects omitted interactions in current sparse estimates, while fresh sparse statistics track input changes and regulate the aging correction. The resulting predictions then renew the sources for whole-step prediction reuse, allowing prediction history to advance between dense refreshes. We evaluate RECAP on multiple video diffusion models against sparse-attention methods, caching methods, and their direct compositions. On HunyuanVideo at 720p 5-second generation, RECAP achieves a 3.05x speedup and outperforms all tested direct compositions in both fidelity and inference speed.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos but remain computationally expensive. Sparse attention and temporal caching reduce computation within and across denoising steps, respectively, offering a natural composition. However, predictions produced with sparse attention become sources for temporal reuse, coupling their errors along the sampling trajectory. We observe that improving the accuracy of these sources recovers fidelity without changing the reuse schedule, and that sparsification errors remain directionally aligned across nearby steps, suggesting that correction information can be shared rather than recomputed. These observations motivate RECAP, a training-free method that couples sparse attention with hierarchical caching through reciprocal correction, adaptively coordinated by online error feedback. Cached dense refinement corrects omitted interactions in current sparse estimates, while fresh sparse statistics track input changes and regulate the aging correction. The resulting predictions then renew the sources for whole-step prediction reuse, allowing prediction history to advance between dense refreshes. We evaluate RECAP on multiple video diffusion models against sparse-attention methods, caching methods, and their direct compositions. On HunyuanVideo at 720p 5-second generation, RECAP achieves a 3.05x speedup and outperforms all tested direct compositions in both fidelity and inference speed.

</details>

## 85. ExMR: Extremity Motion Refinement for Monocular Human Motion Capture

**中文标题**: ExMR: Extremity Motion Refinement for Monocular Human Motion Capture

**作者**: Tom Wehrbein, Bodo Rosenhahn

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26213v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26213v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26213v1)

**项目主页**: https://twehrbein.github.io/exmr-website/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Detailed hand and foot articulation is important for animation and motion analysis, yet remains challenging to recover from monocular video. Recent expressive video methods infer hand articulation from shared whole-body representations. Our conference work suggests that this underexploits local evidence: the Foot Motion Refinement (FootMR) module refines ankle rotations from foot keypoints, improving reconstruction even when the initial estimator receives the same keypoints. We extend this principle to hands with the Hand Motion Refinement (HandMR) module. Because 2D finger keypoints are often missing or unreliable, HandMR combines image features of a pretrained hand reconstruction model with initial arm and hand rotations to refine elbows, wrists, and fingers. We integrate FootMR and HandMR into ExMR (Extremity Motion Refinement), a system for reconstructing 3D body, hand, and foot motion from monocular video. An initial temporal estimator predicts whole-body motion, after which both refiners revisit local evidence to predict residual joint rotations. Experiments on ARCTIC, MOYO, RICH, and MOOF show that ExMR outperforms state-of-the-art methods in hand and foot reconstruction, reducing hand per-vertex error and ankle joint angle error on MOYO by 19% and 18%, respectively, relative to the strongest competitor. Code will be made available for research purposes. Project page: https://twehrbein.github.io/exmr-website/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Detailed hand and foot articulation is important for animation and motion analysis, yet remains challenging to recover from monocular video. Recent expressive video methods infer hand articulation from shared whole-body representations. Our conference work suggests that this underexploits local evidence: the Foot Motion Refinement (FootMR) module refines ankle rotations from foot keypoints, improving reconstruction even when the initial estimator receives the same keypoints. We extend this principle to hands with the Hand Motion Refinement (HandMR) module. Because 2D finger keypoints are often missing or unreliable, HandMR combines image features of a pretrained hand reconstruction model with initial arm and hand rotations to refine elbows, wrists, and fingers. We integrate FootMR and HandMR into ExMR (Extremity Motion Refinement), a system for reconstructing 3D body, hand, and foot motion from monocular video. An initial temporal estimator predicts whole-body motion, after which both refiners revisit local evidence to predict residual joint rotations. Experiments on ARCTIC, MOYO, RICH, and MOOF show that ExMR outperforms state-of-the-art methods in hand and foot reconstruction, reducing hand per-vertex error and ankle joint angle error on MOYO by 19% and 18%, respectively, relative to the strongest competitor. Code will be made available for research purposes. Project page: https://twehrbein.github.io/exmr-website/

</details>

## 86. Wan-Alpha++: Animating a Single RGBA Asset into Controllable Transparent Video Effects

**中文标题**: Wan-Alpha++: Animating a Single RGBA Asset into Controllable Transparent Video Effects

**作者**: Haotian Dong, Xin Wang, Wenjing Wang, Chen Li, Jing Lyu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26189v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26189v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26189v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

RGBA video generation is essential for creating compositable visual assets in filmmaking, game development, and digital content production. However, generating high-quality RGB appearance alongside temporally coherent transparency remains a significant challenge. Existing text-to-RGBA video methods often entangle RGB and alpha distributions and offer limited control over the precise appearance of a target asset. In addition, incorporating image conditioning typically suffers from the emergence-disappearance effects, which fail existing first-frame or first-and-last-frame conditions due to their fully transparent boundary frames. To address these issues, we present Wan-Alpha++, a unified framework that brings controllable RGBA video generation to both text- and image-to-video diffusion backbones through a shared RGBA latent space, extending our conference version Wan-Alpha from the text-conditioned setting to image-conditioned generation with an interior reference frame. Our method first learns shiftable RGB-alpha distributions while preserving the pretrained RGB prior. Specifically, we introduce a transparency-aware bidirectional diffusion objective to produce distinguishable RGBA latent representations, and a transparency-guided mean sampler that shifts the noise distribution to improve transparency generation and enable spatial controllability. Building upon this RGBA generation prior, we introduce temporal phase reparameterization for image-conditioned effect generation. By cyclically transforming a zero-positive-zero alpha pattern into a positive-zero-positive pattern, it relocates visible content to both temporal boundaries, facilitating effective appearance conditioning with limited RGBA data.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

RGBA video generation is essential for creating compositable visual assets in filmmaking, game development, and digital content production. However, generating high-quality RGB appearance alongside temporally coherent transparency remains a significant challenge. Existing text-to-RGBA video methods often entangle RGB and alpha distributions and offer limited control over the precise appearance of a target asset. In addition, incorporating image conditioning typically suffers from the emergence-disappearance effects, which fail existing first-frame or first-and-last-frame conditions due to their fully transparent boundary frames. To address these issues, we present Wan-Alpha++, a unified framework that brings controllable RGBA video generation to both text- and image-to-video diffusion backbones through a shared RGBA latent space, extending our conference version Wan-Alpha from the text-conditioned setting to image-conditioned generation with an interior reference frame. Our method first learns shiftable RGB-alpha distributions while preserving the pretrained RGB prior. Specifically, we introduce a transparency-aware bidirectional diffusion objective to produce distinguishable RGBA latent representations, and a transparency-guided mean sampler that shifts the noise distribution to improve transparency generation and enable spatial controllability. Building upon this RGBA generation prior, we introduce temporal phase reparameterization for image-conditioned effect generation. By cyclically transforming a zero-positive-zero alpha pattern into a positive-zero-positive pattern, it relocates visible content to both temporal boundaries, facilitating effective appearance conditioning with limited RGBA data.

</details>

## 87. TriTD: Tri-Partite Trajectory-Distribution Distillation for Real-Time Autoregressive Video Generation

**中文标题**: TriTD: Tri-Partite Trajectory-Distribution Distillation for Real-Time Autoregressive Video Generation

**作者**: Jia Li, Xurui Peng, Haowei Zhu, Tianyu Zhao, Jiexi Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26165v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26165v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26165v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autoregressive (AR) video diffusion has emerged as a leading paradigm for efficient video generation, yet its practical deployment remains bottlenecked by the trade-off between generation quality and inference efficiency. We attribute this bottleneck to three error sources in AR rollouts: the base model error, the train-inference distribution mismatch, and the upstream error propagation. Building on this decomposition, we derive closed-form upper bounds on the per-step and cumulative errors, providing a principled basis for jointly optimizing generation quality and inference efficiency. Guided by this analysis, we propose TriTD, a Tri-Partite Trajectory-Distribution co-optimized distillation framework with three complementary modules. First, for ODE initialization, we adopt a Next-Node Trajectory Distillation (NTD) objective, which shortens the supervision horizon and admits a lower regression-error floor. Second, we introduce Chunk-Graded Noising (CGN), a monotonically non-decreasing per-chunk noising schedule that helps narrow the expected train-inference noise mismatch and removes redundant KV-cache updates, yielding substantial speedups at no quality cost. Third, we propose Orthogonal-Parallel DMD (OP-DMD), a distribution-matching loss that retains the original DMD supervision while introducing an additional regression constraint in the orthogonal subspace to suppress off-axis drift, improving both training stability and final generation quality. Extensive experiments show that TriTD consistently surpasses existing baselines in visual quality while reaching a real-time throughput of 26.8 FPS on a single H100 GPU, delivering a balanced quality-efficiency solution for interactive streaming video generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autoregressive (AR) video diffusion has emerged as a leading paradigm for efficient video generation, yet its practical deployment remains bottlenecked by the trade-off between generation quality and inference efficiency. We attribute this bottleneck to three error sources in AR rollouts: the base model error, the train-inference distribution mismatch, and the upstream error propagation. Building on this decomposition, we derive closed-form upper bounds on the per-step and cumulative errors, providing a principled basis for jointly optimizing generation quality and inference efficiency. Guided by this analysis, we propose TriTD, a Tri-Partite Trajectory-Distribution co-optimized distillation framework with three complementary modules. First, for ODE initialization, we adopt a Next-Node Trajectory Distillation (NTD) objective, which shortens the supervision horizon and admits a lower regression-error floor. Second, we introduce Chunk-Graded Noising (CGN), a monotonically non-decreasing per-chunk noising schedule that helps narrow the expected train-inference noise mismatch and removes redundant KV-cache updates, yielding substantial speedups at no quality cost. Third, we propose Orthogonal-Parallel DMD (OP-DMD), a distribution-matching loss that retains the original DMD supervision while introducing an additional regression constraint in the orthogonal subspace to suppress off-axis drift, improving both training stability and final generation quality. Extensive experiments show that TriTD consistently surpasses existing baselines in visual quality while reaching a real-time throughput of 26.8 FPS on a single H100 GPU, delivering a balanced quality-efficiency solution for interactive streaming video generation.

</details>

## 88. DeOverlay: A Benchmark and Source-Anchored Flow for Video Overlay Removal

**中文标题**: DeOverlay: A Benchmark and Source-Anchored Flow for Video Overlay Removal

**作者**: Deshui Miao, Yuhang Ma, Shaoyong Jia, Wenzhao Gao, Yue Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26081v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26081v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26081v1)

**项目主页**: https://yahooo-m.github.io/DVTE-Bench/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video overlay removal aims to recover scenes obscured by subtitles, titles, and logos while preserving surrounding content. Existing methods use temporal propagation or source-conditioned generation, but can leave residual overlays and distort scene structure when useful source information is obscured. We propose DeOverlay, a source-anchored flow model that reconstructs the clean video from the source latent with fixed source guidance. Specifically, we train a shared velocity network along paired source-to-clean paths using region-weighted flow matching, enabling successive cor- rections from intermediate states. To select local source cues, we develop visibility-normalized temporal aggregation that excludes masked positions and averages the remaining observations. Global source context and edit-region embeddings complement these cues through additive condi- tioning, retaining a single video-token sequence. To evaluate scene recovery across diverse overlay conditions, we introduce DVTE-Bench, comprising 1,631 paired videos across eight text-overlay categories, with OCR-screened clean targets, frame-aligned pixel masks, and a common evaluation protocol. Experiments on the complete release show that DeOverlay outperforms four baselines on all five reconstruction and perceptual metrics, reducing Mask LPIPS and Mask VFID by 58.3% and 70.3%, respectively, relative to the strongest evaluated baseline. Additional qualitative examples demonstrate the removal of graphic logos and stickers. We will make the complete benchmark, evaluation code, and test outputs availabl. Project page:https://yahooo-m.github.io/DVTE-Bench/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video overlay removal aims to recover scenes obscured by subtitles, titles, and logos while preserving surrounding content. Existing methods use temporal propagation or source-conditioned generation, but can leave residual overlays and distort scene structure when useful source information is obscured. We propose DeOverlay, a source-anchored flow model that reconstructs the clean video from the source latent with fixed source guidance. Specifically, we train a shared velocity network along paired source-to-clean paths using region-weighted flow matching, enabling successive cor- rections from intermediate states. To select local source cues, we develop visibility-normalized temporal aggregation that excludes masked positions and averages the remaining observations. Global source context and edit-region embeddings complement these cues through additive condi- tioning, retaining a single video-token sequence. To evaluate scene recovery across diverse overlay conditions, we introduce DVTE-Bench, comprising 1,631 paired videos across eight text-overlay categories, with OCR-screened clean targets, frame-aligned pixel masks, and a common evaluation protocol. Experiments on the complete release show that DeOverlay outperforms four baselines on all five reconstruction and perceptual metrics, reducing Mask LPIPS and Mask VFID by 58.3% and 70.3%, respectively, relative to the strongest evaluated baseline. Additional qualitative examples demonstrate the removal of graphic logos and stickers. We will make the complete benchmark, evaluation code, and test outputs availabl. Project page:https://yahooo-m.github.io/DVTE-Bench/

</details>

## 89. Risk Decomposition for Hallucination-Aware Video Sampling

**中文标题**: Risk Decomposition for Hallucination-Aware Video Sampling

**作者**: Chenyan Zheng, Fan Wu, Ruibo Li, Jiaxing Li, Minghao Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26069v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26069v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26069v2)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Despite achieving high visual fidelity, video models still suffer from physical hallucinations, limiting their reliability as world simulators. Existing methods leverage model-internal or external signals to improve physical plausibility, but their roles and limitations require a unified explanation. We propose a two-dimensional physical-hallucination risk framework that defines low-density sampling risk and high-density drifting risk. The first risk captures generations assigned low likelihood by the video model, for example, a ball suddenly teleporting despite the model favoring continuous motion, while the second risk captures physical inconsistencies in high-likelihood generations, for example, a ball bouncing progressively higher without additional energy. Our analysis explains how model-internal signals steer sampling toward higher-density regions of the learned distribution to address low-density sampling risk. However, higher model likelihood does not guarantee physical plausibility, motivating external guidance to address high-density drifting risk. Based on this framework, we develop Hallucination-Aware Sampler (HA-Sampler), which applies external physical guidance within latent refinement to generate physically plausible videos, and HA-Scorer, which derives a likelihood-based signal from the generator itself for evaluating generated videos using continuous normalizing flow (CNF), with coarse ODE integration and finite-difference divergence estimates. Experiments on Physics-IQ and VideoPhy benchmarks support our theoretical analysis and demonstrate that our methods outperform the compared sampling and scoring SOTA baselines.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Despite achieving high visual fidelity, video models still suffer from physical hallucinations, limiting their reliability as world simulators. Existing methods leverage model-internal or external signals to improve physical plausibility, but their roles and limitations require a unified explanation. We propose a two-dimensional physical-hallucination risk framework that defines low-density sampling risk and high-density drifting risk. The first risk captures generations assigned low likelihood by the video model, for example, a ball suddenly teleporting despite the model favoring continuous motion, while the second risk captures physical inconsistencies in high-likelihood generations, for example, a ball bouncing progressively higher without additional energy. Our analysis explains how model-internal signals steer sampling toward higher-density regions of the learned distribution to address low-density sampling risk. However, higher model likelihood does not guarantee physical plausibility, motivating external guidance to address high-density drifting risk. Based on this framework, we develop Hallucination-Aware Sampler (HA-Sampler), which applies external physical guidance within latent refinement to generate physically plausible videos, and HA-Scorer, which derives a likelihood-based signal from the generator itself for evaluating generated videos using continuous normalizing flow (CNF), with coarse ODE integration and finite-difference divergence estimates. Experiments on Physics-IQ and VideoPhy benchmarks support our theoretical analysis and demonstrate that our methods outperform the compared sampling and scoring SOTA baselines.

</details>

## 90. AVTime: Reinforcing Dense Audio-Visual Captions with Bidirectional Alignment

**中文标题**: AVTime: Reinforcing Dense Audio-Visual Captions with Bidirectional Alignment

**作者**: Mingyang Wu, Kaituo Feng, Bohao Li, Kaixiong Gong, Zihao Yin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26046v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26046v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26046v1)

**项目主页**: https://huggingface.co/collections/Apryle/avtime.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dense temporal audio-visual captioning is important for multimodal video understanding and generation. However, prior work is constrained by three main limitations: (1) the lack of a dataset combining high temporal resolution, longer videos, and bidirectional alignment between time and events; (2) the reliance of most existing RL methods on checklists; and (3) the lack of a benchmark that bidirectionally evaluates event semantic correctness and temporal accuracy. To address these challenges, we propose: (1) AVTime-50K, a high-quality 50K-scale dataset with temporally precise, semantically accurate captions and longer videos; (2) AVTime, a state-of-the-art model optimized with a novel Bidirectional Temporal-Semantic Alignment (BiTSA) reward that imposes explicit bidirectional constraints between time and events; and (3) AVTime-Bench, a specialized benchmark that introduces a novel bidirectional evaluation of temporal accuracy and semantic correctness. Our code, models, and datasets are available at https://huggingface.co/collections/Apryle/avtime.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dense temporal audio-visual captioning is important for multimodal video understanding and generation. However, prior work is constrained by three main limitations: (1) the lack of a dataset combining high temporal resolution, longer videos, and bidirectional alignment between time and events; (2) the reliance of most existing RL methods on checklists; and (3) the lack of a benchmark that bidirectionally evaluates event semantic correctness and temporal accuracy. To address these challenges, we propose: (1) AVTime-50K, a high-quality 50K-scale dataset with temporally precise, semantically accurate captions and longer videos; (2) AVTime, a state-of-the-art model optimized with a novel Bidirectional Temporal-Semantic Alignment (BiTSA) reward that imposes explicit bidirectional constraints between time and events; and (3) AVTime-Bench, a specialized benchmark that introduces a novel bidirectional evaluation of temporal accuracy and semantic correctness. Our code, models, and datasets are available at https://huggingface.co/collections/Apryle/avtime.

</details>

## 91. TamedFlow: Trajectory-Aware Distillation for Teacher-Assisted Diffusion Sampling

**中文标题**: TamedFlow: Trajectory-Aware Distillation for Teacher-Assisted Diffusion Sampling

**作者**: Shuaiqi Cheng, Jiahao Zhang, Boyuan Gu, Yuxi Chen, Haiying Xu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26025v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26025v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26025v2)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) deliver high-fidelity image and video generation, but inference requires many sequential model evaluations. Two acceleration strategies are increasingly important: model compression makes each evaluation cheaper, while adaptive inference avoids selected teacher computation through reuse, forecasting, or speculative execution. Their combination, however, is brittle: directly applying the evaluated adaptive methods to a compressed student can degrade quality, an observed failure mode we call the \textbf{Combination Gap}. We link this gap to a mismatch between pointwise distillation and short-horizon execution: a student trained at teacher states need not remain reliable when its own predictions are chained. We propose \textbf{TamedFlow}, which trains a compressed DiT for this deployed role through cross-architecture feature alignment, a near-identity Velocity Alignment Head, and randomized rollout supervision. At inference, a lightweight Learnable Trajectory Estimator routes completed drafts: accepted drafts bypass the teacher, while rejected drafts roll back and receive one teacher rescue step. Distilling DiT-XL/2 into DiT-B/2, TamedFlow reaches FID $2.58$ at $2.25\times$ measured speedup over DDIM-50 on ImageNet $256\times256$. Ablations support trajectory supervision, and routing controls favor LTE over random decisions at nearly matched latency. Evaluations on ImageNet $512\times512$, MS-COCO, and Latte/FaceForensics extend the empirical scope.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Diffusion Transformers (DiTs) deliver high-fidelity image and video generation, but inference requires many sequential model evaluations. Two acceleration strategies are increasingly important: model compression makes each evaluation cheaper, while adaptive inference avoids selected teacher computation through reuse, forecasting, or speculative execution. Their combination, however, is brittle: directly applying the evaluated adaptive methods to a compressed student can degrade quality, an observed failure mode we call the \textbf{Combination Gap}. We link this gap to a mismatch between pointwise distillation and short-horizon execution: a student trained at teacher states need not remain reliable when its own predictions are chained. We propose \textbf{TamedFlow}, which trains a compressed DiT for this deployed role through cross-architecture feature alignment, a near-identity Velocity Alignment Head, and randomized rollout supervision. At inference, a lightweight Learnable Trajectory Estimator routes completed drafts: accepted drafts bypass the teacher, while rejected drafts roll back and receive one teacher rescue step. Distilling DiT-XL/2 into DiT-B/2, TamedFlow reaches FID $2.58$ at $2.25\times$ measured speedup over DDIM-50 on ImageNet $256\times256$. Ablations support trajectory supervision, and routing controls favor LTE over random decisions at nearly matched latency. Evaluations on ImageNet $512\times512$, MS-COCO, and Latte/FaceForensics extend the empirical scope.

</details>

## 92. FrameGRPO: Incentivizing Video Reasoning via Multi-Frame Rollouts and Temporal Credit

**中文标题**: FrameGRPO: Incentivizing Video Reasoning via Multi-Frame Rollouts and Temporal Credit

**作者**: Jiaxuan Sun, Biao Wang, Shuai Li

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26022v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26022v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26022v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards, particularly Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), has improved video reasoning in multimodal large language models. However, standard video GRPO varies only linguistic trajectories while conditioning every rollout in a group on the same sampled frames, which can produce homogeneous rewards and weaken group-relative learning when the shared visual evidence is insufficient or distracting. We propose \textbf{FrameGRPO}, a simple and effective reinforcement-learning framework that requires neither auxiliary networks nor changes to the model architecture. Specifically, FrameGRPO combines two lightweight components: (1) \textbf{Multi-Frame Rollouts}, which use only uniform temporal subsampling of a single maximal-frame decode to construct aligned views that diversify visual evidence and reasoning trajectories; and (2) \textbf{Gated Temporal Efficiency Credit}, which selectively reinforces a correct lower-ratio trajectory when all rollouts in a fixed full-ratio reference subset fail, without imposing an unconditional preference for fewer frames. Extensive experiments across diverse video understanding and reasoning benchmarks demonstrate robust overall improvements over standard GRPO, highlighting temporal-view diversification as an effective mechanism for exploration and credit assignment in video MLLMs.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards, particularly Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO), has improved video reasoning in multimodal large language models. However, standard video GRPO varies only linguistic trajectories while conditioning every rollout in a group on the same sampled frames, which can produce homogeneous rewards and weaken group-relative learning when the shared visual evidence is insufficient or distracting. We propose \textbf{FrameGRPO}, a simple and effective reinforcement-learning framework that requires neither auxiliary networks nor changes to the model architecture. Specifically, FrameGRPO combines two lightweight components: (1) \textbf{Multi-Frame Rollouts}, which use only uniform temporal subsampling of a single maximal-frame decode to construct aligned views that diversify visual evidence and reasoning trajectories; and (2) \textbf{Gated Temporal Efficiency Credit}, which selectively reinforces a correct lower-ratio trajectory when all rollouts in a fixed full-ratio reference subset fail, without imposing an unconditional preference for fewer frames. Extensive experiments across diverse video understanding and reasoning benchmarks demonstrate robust overall improvements over standard GRPO, highlighting temporal-view diversification as an effective mechanism for exploration and credit assignment in video MLLMs.

</details>

## 93. HORIA: 4D Human and Object Reconstruction from a Monocular Video with Interaction-Aware Re-Rendering

**中文标题**: HORIA: 4D Human and Object Reconstruction from a Monocular Video with Interaction-Aware Re-Rendering

**作者**: Hyeongjin Nam, Taeryung Lee, Daniel Sungho Jung, Kyoung Mu Lee

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25960v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25960v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25960v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Joint 4D reconstruction of a human and object from a monocular video is essential for understanding how people interact with their environment. While recent methods have made noticeable progress, capturing such human-object interactions remains difficult due to two main challenges. First, a video typically contains composite semantic context spanning multiple interaction phases, making it difficult to track the temporal changes of the interaction in the reconstruction. Second, under human-object occlusion, a single-view observation contains incomplete visual information about the interaction, resulting in geometrically inconsistent reconstructions. To address these challenges, we introduce HORIA, built upon two core designs. First, we decompose the entire input video into temporally grounded and semantically distinct interaction phases with motion-aware captioning, obtaining a specific text description for each phase. Second, conditioned on these text descriptions of the interaction, we propose an interaction-aware re-rendering that compensates for the limited single-view observation. Specifically, we render the initially reconstructed 4D human and object from novel viewpoints and refine the renderings with a text-to-video diffusion prior, which provides auxiliary novel-view supervision beyond single-view cues. As a result, our framework produces temporally coherent and accurate 4D reconstructions under diverse interaction scenarios, achieving state-of-the-art performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Joint 4D reconstruction of a human and object from a monocular video is essential for understanding how people interact with their environment. While recent methods have made noticeable progress, capturing such human-object interactions remains difficult due to two main challenges. First, a video typically contains composite semantic context spanning multiple interaction phases, making it difficult to track the temporal changes of the interaction in the reconstruction. Second, under human-object occlusion, a single-view observation contains incomplete visual information about the interaction, resulting in geometrically inconsistent reconstructions. To address these challenges, we introduce HORIA, built upon two core designs. First, we decompose the entire input video into temporally grounded and semantically distinct interaction phases with motion-aware captioning, obtaining a specific text description for each phase. Second, conditioned on these text descriptions of the interaction, we propose an interaction-aware re-rendering that compensates for the limited single-view observation. Specifically, we render the initially reconstructed 4D human and object from novel viewpoints and refine the renderings with a text-to-video diffusion prior, which provides auxiliary novel-view supervision beyond single-view cues. As a result, our framework produces temporally coherent and accurate 4D reconstructions under diverse interaction scenarios, achieving state-of-the-art performance.

</details>

## 94. OmniGround: Strengthening Omni-Modal Temporal Grounding via Attention-Consistency Training

**中文标题**: OmniGround: Strengthening Omni-Modal Temporal Grounding via Attention-Consistency Training

**作者**: Zixuan Chen, Depeng Wang, Hao Lin, Ke Xu, Ya Guo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25929v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25929v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25929v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Omni-modal large language models can jointly understand video, audio, and text, yet they remain weak at temporal grounding, a capability essential for real-world audio-visual applications. To understand this limitation, we look beyond final predictions and examine the model's internal temporal evidence. We identify a temporal generation gap: the internal attention of the model localizes the target moment more accurately than the timestamps it generates. To close this gap, we propose TACG (Temporal Attention-Consistency Grounding), a post-training method that treats internal attention as a reliable temporal pivot and aligns it with both the model prediction and the ground-truth annotation through attention-consistency training. We further build a 580K-sample training dataset by reconstructing temporal QA from dense audio-visual annotations and organizing general omni-modal instruction data. Using TACG and this training dataset, we train OmniGround, a general-purpose omni-modal model with stronger temporal grounding capability, without injecting any additional temporal cues. OmniGround reaches state-of-the-art omni-modal temporal grounding, including 37.5 mIoU on LongVALE and 35.7 mIoU on UnAV-100, while staying competitive on both general audio-visual understanding and vision-only grounding.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Omni-modal large language models can jointly understand video, audio, and text, yet they remain weak at temporal grounding, a capability essential for real-world audio-visual applications. To understand this limitation, we look beyond final predictions and examine the model's internal temporal evidence. We identify a temporal generation gap: the internal attention of the model localizes the target moment more accurately than the timestamps it generates. To close this gap, we propose TACG (Temporal Attention-Consistency Grounding), a post-training method that treats internal attention as a reliable temporal pivot and aligns it with both the model prediction and the ground-truth annotation through attention-consistency training. We further build a 580K-sample training dataset by reconstructing temporal QA from dense audio-visual annotations and organizing general omni-modal instruction data. Using TACG and this training dataset, we train OmniGround, a general-purpose omni-modal model with stronger temporal grounding capability, without injecting any additional temporal cues. OmniGround reaches state-of-the-art omni-modal temporal grounding, including 37.5 mIoU on LongVALE and 35.7 mIoU on UnAV-100, while staying competitive on both general audio-visual understanding and vision-only grounding.

</details>
