## 95. SAPO: Self-Supervised Attention Policy Optimization for Improving Temporal Video Grounding in Vision-Language Models

**中文标题**: SAPO: Self-Supervised Attention Policy Optimization for Improving Temporal Video Grounding in Vision-Language Models

**作者**: Zixuan Chen, Depeng Wang, Wei Chen, Ke Xu, Ya Guo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25923v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25923v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25923v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video vision-language models (VLMs) have strong semantic understanding, yet remain unreliable at precise temporal grounding. Existing gains from explicitly aligning video frames with absolute time suggest that VLMs may already encode accurate temporal locations internally but lose precision when expressing them as timestamps. Our analysis across four VLMs and two grounding datasets confirms this hypothesis: attention intervals outperform predicted intervals on approximately 66% of valid examples. We term this discrepancy the temporal generation gap. Because precise temporal annotations are expensive, this finding motivates closing the gap to improve temporal grounding using only unlabeled video-query pairs. We propose Self-Supervised Attention Policy Optimization (SAPO), which uses internal temporal attention as self-supervision for Group Relative Policy Optimization and applies a ground-truth-free criterion to select high-value samples. SAPO consistently improves temporal grounding across multiple datasets and models spanning weak to strong initial grounding ability. Relative mIoU gains reach 33.7% on Qwen2.5-VL-3B and 5.4% on the strong Time-R1-7B. The improvements transfer across datasets while preserving general video understanding.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video vision-language models (VLMs) have strong semantic understanding, yet remain unreliable at precise temporal grounding. Existing gains from explicitly aligning video frames with absolute time suggest that VLMs may already encode accurate temporal locations internally but lose precision when expressing them as timestamps. Our analysis across four VLMs and two grounding datasets confirms this hypothesis: attention intervals outperform predicted intervals on approximately 66% of valid examples. We term this discrepancy the temporal generation gap. Because precise temporal annotations are expensive, this finding motivates closing the gap to improve temporal grounding using only unlabeled video-query pairs. We propose Self-Supervised Attention Policy Optimization (SAPO), which uses internal temporal attention as self-supervision for Group Relative Policy Optimization and applies a ground-truth-free criterion to select high-value samples. SAPO consistently improves temporal grounding across multiple datasets and models spanning weak to strong initial grounding ability. Relative mIoU gains reach 33.7% on Qwen2.5-VL-3B and 5.4% on the strong Time-R1-7B. The improvements transfer across datasets while preserving general video understanding.

</details>

## 96. MAPS: A Multi-Agent Personalized Script-Driven Video Advertising System Enhanced by Reinforcement Learning

**中文标题**: MAPS: A Multi-Agent Personalized Script-Driven Video Advertising System Enhanced by Reinforcement Learning

**作者**: Zhongyu Wang, Chen Zuo, Qingxuan Sun, Jiayang Qiao, Qiuhao Qian et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25748v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25748v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25748v1)

**类别**: cs.IR, cs.CV, cs.MA

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

With the rapid growth of online video consumption, video advertising has become a dominant component of digital advertising. Most personalized video advertising systems follow a retrieval-based paradigm, which matches ad content with user preferences in a feature space and recommends the most similar candidates. However, since the candidate creatives are pre-produced, this paradigm limits the granularity of personalization and leads to performance disparities across diverse user groups. To address this limitation, we propose a multi-agent personalized script-driven video advertising system (MAPS). We design four collaborative agents that form a closed-loop pipeline of user profile modeling, script generation, video synthesis, and feedback-driven iterative refinement. To train the Scripter Agent, we develop a reinforcement learning framework equipped with a multi-dimensional reward function, which steers the agent toward producing scripts that simultaneously align with user content preferences and target product information. Extensive offline experiments on representative datasets demonstrate that our approach achieves encouraging performance in generating and integrating personalized creatives. Online A/B experiments in a specific scenario in the Kuaishou advertising system show that MAPS improves CTR by 2.87%, CVR by 2.74%, and ARPU by 9.85% over the generative baseline.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

With the rapid growth of online video consumption, video advertising has become a dominant component of digital advertising. Most personalized video advertising systems follow a retrieval-based paradigm, which matches ad content with user preferences in a feature space and recommends the most similar candidates. However, since the candidate creatives are pre-produced, this paradigm limits the granularity of personalization and leads to performance disparities across diverse user groups. To address this limitation, we propose a multi-agent personalized script-driven video advertising system (MAPS). We design four collaborative agents that form a closed-loop pipeline of user profile modeling, script generation, video synthesis, and feedback-driven iterative refinement. To train the Scripter Agent, we develop a reinforcement learning framework equipped with a multi-dimensional reward function, which steers the agent toward producing scripts that simultaneously align with user content preferences and target product information. Extensive offline experiments on representative datasets demonstrate that our approach achieves encouraging performance in generating and integrating personalized creatives. Online A/B experiments in a specific scenario in the Kuaishou advertising system show that MAPS improves CTR by 2.87%, CVR by 2.74%, and ARPU by 9.85% over the generative baseline.

</details>

## 97. Frechet Forcing: Training Long-Horizon Streaming World Models from Short Clips

**中文标题**: Frechet Forcing: Training Long-Horizon Streaming World Models from Short Clips

**作者**: Yian Zhao, Yu Yan, Hongcan Guo, Ruochong Zheng, Jian Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25666v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25666v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25666v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Streaming video world models generate the future chunk by chunk, conditioned on their own past and a stream of actions. Trained on short clips, they drift once a rollout exceeds the training horizon, as prediction errors re-enter the context and compound. A common remedy is to train the model on its own rollouts via distribution matching distillation (DMD). For action-conditioned world models, the teacher becomes a bottleneck: an external video model is agnostic to the actions, whereas the model's own pretrained network drifts in the same manner as the student. We observe that a long rollout need not match a teacher, provided it remains a realistic continuation of its own scene that follows the commanded actions. Realism and scene fidelity can be measured against real-data statistics, which remain valid at any horizon, while action following is preserved by the model's conditional generation loss. We propose Fréchet Forcing, which fine-tunes a world model together with its deployed multi-step sampler under three constraints. Generative anchoring applies the flow-matching loss after a self-generated history ending on real data. Context fidelity, a Fréchet distance (FD) on latent statistics of the free rollout centred on each clip's own ground truth, keeps every rollout faithful to its scene. Reality matching, an FD to real frames in a frozen perceptual space, keeps the generated frames realistic. Because all three targets are derived from short real clips without comparing a rollout against a long recorded future, training requires no teacher, no online critic and no long videos. Fine-tuning MiniWorld-1B, pretrained on 253-frame clips, for at most 36 GPU-hours on 381-frame clips yields rollouts that remain stable beyond 4,000 frames and continue to follow their actions in both robot manipulation and camera-controlled exploration.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reality matching, an FD to real frames in a frozen perceptual space, keeps the generated frames realistic. </details>

## 98. PoSTCache: From Forecasting to Postdictive Smoothing for Revisable Diffusion Caching

**中文标题**: PoSTCache: From Forecasting to Postdictive Smoothing for Revisable Diffusion Caching

**作者**: Liwei Lan, Hui Chen, Jungong Han, Guiguang Ding

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25664v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25664v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25664v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Diffusion and flow matching models achieve impressive visual generation quality but remain computationally expensive due to iterative sampling. Caching-based acceleration provides a practical training-free solution by reusing or predicting intermediate features. However, existing methods largely enforce a forward-only information flow: approximations produced for skipped model evaluations are immediately committed to the sampling trajectory and cannot benefit from feedback generated by later full-computation steps. We propose \textbf{PoSTCache} (\emph{Postdictive Smoothing Trajectory Cache}), a training-free and plug-and-play framework that reformulates diffusion caching as revisable trajectory inference. PoSTCache first uses an existing caching strategy to construct forward proposals for skipped steps. At a later full-computation step, the discrepancy between the refreshed prediction and its forward proposal yields a correction signal, which PoSTCache propagates backward to refine and reintegrate the preceding predictions. Viewing full-computation steps as sources of corrective feedback also changes how they should be selected. We therefore introduce the Posterior Variance Reduction (PVR) strategy, which estimates cross-timestep dependencies from offline denoising trajectories and selects anchors according to their expected reduction in surrogate trajectory uncertainty rather than local approximation error alone. Experiments on representative image and video generation models show that PoSTCache consistently improves the quality--efficiency trade-off over representative caching methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Diffusion and flow matching models achieve impressive visual generation quality but remain computationally expensive due to iterative sampling. Caching-based acceleration provides a practical training-free solution by reusing or predicting intermediate features. However, existing methods largely enforce a forward-only information flow: approximations produced for skipped model evaluations are immediately committed to the sampling trajectory and cannot benefit from feedback generated by later full-computation steps. We propose \textbf{PoSTCache} (\emph{Postdictive Smoothing Trajectory Cache}), a training-free and plug-and-play framework that reformulates diffusion caching as revisable trajectory inference. PoSTCache first uses an existing caching strategy to construct forward proposals for skipped steps. At a later full-computation step, the discrepancy between the refreshed prediction and its forward proposal yields a correction signal, which PoSTCache propagates backward to refine and reintegrate the preceding predictions. Viewing full-computation steps as sources of corrective feedback also changes how they should be selected. We therefore introduce the Posterior Variance Reduction (PVR) strategy, which estimates cross-timestep dependencies from offline denoising trajectories and selects anchors according to their expected reduction in surrogate trajectory uncertainty rather than local approximation error alone. Experiments on representative image and video generation models show that PoSTCache consistently improves the quality--efficiency trade-off over representative caching methods.

</details>

## 99. FluidGen: Physics-Aware Fluid Video Generation via Motion-Guided Post-Training

**中文标题**: FluidGen: Physics-Aware Fluid Video Generation via Motion-Guided Post-Training

**作者**: Yang Zheng, Zizhang Li, Boyang Deng, Jan Ackermann, Lei Shu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25660v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25660v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25660v1)

**项目主页**: https://y-zheng18.github.io/FluidGen/

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.GR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video generation models produce visually compelling motion but systematically fail on fluid phenomena, including smoke, fire, and explosion debris, where outputs lack coherent vortical structure and collapse to bland dynamics that violate the physics of real fluids. Classical solvers achieve high physical fidelity but require detailed 3D scenes and carefully tuned parameters, making them impractical for in-the-wild imagery and video generation. We address this gap by injecting a data-driven fluid prior into a pre-trained video model, trained on a mixture of high-fidelity Houdini simulation data with paired velocity-field ground truth and curated in-the-wild fluid video data for appearance diversity. The core of our approach is a dual stream architecture that predicts per-frame velocity fields alongside RGB, supervised by simulation ground truth. This auxiliary signal regularizes generation toward physically consistent motion and exposes an interpretable physical quantity that we further leverage as a reward for velocity-aware direct preference optimization. Our model produces substantially more realistic fluid dynamics sequences than strong video baselines and optimization-based methods. Project page: https://y-zheng18.github.io/FluidGen/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video generation models produce visually compelling motion but systematically fail on fluid phenomena, including smoke, fire, and explosion debris, where outputs lack coherent vortical structure and collapse to bland dynamics that violate the physics of real fluids. Classical solvers achieve high physical fidelity but require detailed 3D scenes and carefully tuned parameters, making them impractical for in-the-wild imagery and video generation. We address this gap by injecting a data-driven fluid prior into a pre-trained video model, trained on a mixture of high-fidelity Houdini simulation data with paired velocity-field ground truth and curated in-the-wild fluid video data for appearance diversity. The core of our approach is a dual stream architecture that predicts per-frame velocity fields alongside RGB, supervised by simulation ground truth. This auxiliary signal regularizes generation toward physically consistent motion and exposes an interpretable physical quantity that we further leverage as a reward for velocity-aware direct preference optimization. Our model produces substantially more realistic fluid dynamics sequences than strong video baselines and optimization-based methods. Project page: https://y-zheng18.github.io/FluidGen/

</details>

## 100. Clean Forcing: Drift-Resistant Autoregressive Video Diffusion with a Frozen Base

**中文标题**: Clean Forcing: Drift-Resistant Autoregressive Video Diffusion with a Frozen Base

**作者**: Wenqing Wang, Joonghyuk Shin, Jonathan Tremblay, Chan Hee Song, Yun Fu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25634v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25634v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25634v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models generate streaming video of arbitrary length by conditioning each new chunk of frames on those already generated. However, they suffer from exposure bias, where a model trained on clean context must continue from its own imperfect outputs, and errors compound until quality collapses within seconds (drift). Existing methods either require cluster-scale base retraining, apply training-free inference corrections with inconsistent results, or train frozen-base correctors for sampling and cache errors. We instead find that drift is largely deterministic and therefore learnable, as holding the noisy state fixed while varying only the history reveals a counterfactual velocity gap that is ~95% systematic across noise seeds. Based on this observation, we introduce Clean Forcing, which trains a 5.9M-parameter LoRA corrector on the frozen base model to regress this gap, with a closed-loop stage that adds DAgger-style aggregation and a drift-contraction objective to expose the corrector to its own rollouts. Because the clean histories can be the base model's own single-shot generations, training needs no real videos, and the merged corrector adds no inference cost. In 50 s text-to-video generation, Clean Forcing reduces Δ-drift from +11.3 to +1.65 without real video and to -0.16 with 40 real clips, improving on the best published result of +3.57 with over 150x less data. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Clean Forcing outperforms prior methods in aesthetic quality and data efficiency with reduced drift for long-horizon video generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models generate streaming video of arbitrary length by conditioning each new chunk of frames on those already generated. However, they suffer from exposure bias, where a model trained on clean context must continue from its own imperfect outputs, and errors compound until quality collapses within seconds (drift). Existing methods either require cluster-scale base retraining, apply training-free inference corrections with inconsistent results, or train frozen-base correctors for sampling and cache errors. We instead find that drift is largely deterministic and therefore learnable, as holding the noisy state fixed while varying only the history reveals a counterfactual velocity gap that is ~95% systematic across noise seeds. Based on this observation, we introduce Clean Forcing, which trains a 5.9M-parameter LoRA corrector on the frozen base model to regress this gap, with a closed-loop stage that adds DAgger-style aggregation and a drift-contraction objective to expose the corrector to its own rollouts. Because the clean histories can be the base model's own single-shot generations, training needs no real videos, and the merged corrector adds no inference cost. In 50 s text-to-video generation, Clean Forcing reduces Δ-drift from +11.3 to +1.65 without real video and to -0.16 with 40 real clips, improving on the best published result of +3.57 with over 150x less data. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Clean Forcing outperforms prior methods in aesthetic quality and data efficiency with reduced drift for long-horizon video generation.

</details>

## 101. Confidence-Guided Pseudo-Label Refinement for Unsupervised Cross-Domain Person Re-Identification

**中文标题**: Confidence-Guided Pseudo-Label Refinement for Unsupervised Cross-Domain Person Re-Identification

**作者**: Sundas Iqbal, Danish Ali, Qing Tian, Yuhui Zheng, Jianping Gou et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25633v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25633v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25633v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/Sundas058/CGPLR.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Unsupervised cross-domain person re-identification remains challenging because domain shifts generate noisy pseudo labels that are reinforced during iterative adaptation. Existing clustering-based methods often rely on limited reliability cues or global confidence criteria and may impose hard identity supervision on ambiguous target samples. To address these limitations, we propose CGPLR, a confidence-guided pseudo-label refinement framework for unsupervised domain-adaptive person re-identification. CGPLR estimates sample-level reliability by jointly modeling centroid compatibility, local neighborhood consistency, separation from the strongest competing cluster and temporal assignment stability. The resulting confidence distribution is used to derive cluster-adaptive upper and lower thresholds for a three-way selective decision. Reliable assignments are preserved, recoverable samples are reassigned only when an alternative cluster receives sufficient geometric and neighborhood support and highly ambiguous samples are deferred from hard identity supervision. The learning objective adapts to these decisions through confidence-weighted supervision for preserved samples, cautious corrected-label weighting for reassigned samples and label-free representation consistency for deferred samples. CGPLR achieves 76.4% Rank-1 accuracy and 47.6% mean average precision (mAP) on Market-1501 to MSMT17, and 95.1% Rank-1 accuracy and 87.7% mAP on MSMT17 to Market-1501, demonstrating effective reliability-aware selective adaptation. The code is available at https://github.com/Sundas058/CGPLR.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Unsupervised cross-domain person re-identification remains challenging because domain shifts generate noisy pseudo labels that are reinforced during iterative adaptation. Existing clustering-based methods often rely on limited reliability cues or global confidence criteria and may impose hard identity supervision on ambiguous target samples. To address these limitations, we propose CGPLR, a confidence-guided pseudo-label refinement framework for unsupervised domain-adaptive person re-identification. CGPLR estimates sample-level reliability by jointly modeling centroid compatibility, local neighborhood consistency, separation from the strongest competing cluster and temporal assignment stability. The resulting confidence distribution is used to derive cluster-adaptive upper and lower thresholds for a three-way selective decision. Reliable assignments are preserved, recoverable samples are reassigned only when an alternative cluster receives sufficient geometric and neighborhood support and highly ambiguous samples are deferred from hard identity supervision. The learning objective adapts to these decisions through confidence-weighted supervision for preserved samples, cautious corrected-label weighting for reassigned samples and label-free representation consistency for deferred samples. CGPLR achieves 76.4% Rank-1 accuracy and 47.6% mean average precision (mAP) on Market-1501 to MSMT17, and 95.1% Rank-1 accuracy and 87.7% mAP on MSMT17 to Market-1501, demonstrating effective reliability-aware selective adaptation. The code is available at https://github.com/Sundas058/CGPLR.

</details>

## 102. Recurrent Vision Transformers for Event-Based Spacecraft Pose Estimation Under Slow Relative Motions

**中文标题**: Recurrent Vision Transformers for Event-Based Spacecraft Pose Estimation Under Slow Relative Motions

**作者**: Sofia McLeod, Mohsi Jawaid, Carl Seubert, Tat-Jun Chin

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25622v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25622v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25622v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-based object pose estimation is vital for conducting spacecraft rendezvous and proximity operations (RPO). Recent research has demonstrated the robustness of event cameras against the harsh lighting conditions that often occur in orbit, thanks to the extremely high dynamic range of event sensors. However, event-based spacecraft pose estimation (EBSPE) suffers when the relative motion between the chaser and target is slow, since the event rate drops significantly, leading to data with low signal-to-noise ratio. Unfortunately, for safety reasons, real RPO trajectories often involve slow relative motions (e.g., down to 1 mm/s), and operating under low illumination exacerbates the problem. Addressing slow relative motions is thus crucial to raise the practicality of EBSPE. To this end, we propose using recurrent vision transformers (RVT) for EBSPE. The key idea is to exploit recurrent memory to capture longer-term information across sparse event streams, and to learn the temporal attention span from full trajectories rather than fixing the time window. We developed an RVT-based heatmap regressor to produce 2D-3D correspondences for pose estimation, which was validated on real event data collected under slow relative motions representative of RPO trajectories. Results indicate the superior pose estimation accuracy and stability of our RVT-based method, relative to previous per-event-frame-based or fixed-time-window EBSPE methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-based object pose estimation is vital for conducting spacecraft rendezvous and proximity operations (RPO). Recent research has demonstrated the robustness of event cameras against the harsh lighting conditions that often occur in orbit, thanks to the extremely high dynamic range of event sensors. However, event-based spacecraft pose estimation (EBSPE) suffers when the relative motion between the chaser and target is slow, since the event rate drops significantly, leading to data with low signal-to-noise ratio. Unfortunately, for safety reasons, real RPO trajectories often involve slow relative motions (e.g., down to 1 mm/s), and operating under low illumination exacerbates the problem. Addressing slow relative motions is thus crucial to raise the practicality of EBSPE. To this end, we propose using recurrent vision transformers (RVT) for EBSPE. The key idea is to exploit recurrent memory to capture longer-term information across sparse event streams, and to learn the temporal attention span from full trajectories rather than fixing the time window. We developed an RVT-based heatmap regressor to produce 2D-3D correspondences for pose estimation, which was validated on real event data collected under slow relative motions representative of RPO trajectories. Results indicate the superior pose estimation accuracy and stability of our RVT-based method, relative to previous per-event-frame-based or fixed-time-window EBSPE methods.

</details>

## 103. Modeling Authentic Research Trajectories: A Graph View of Research Ideation

**中文标题**: Modeling Authentic Research Trajectories: A Graph View of Research Ideation

**作者**: Ruochen Li, Zhiyu Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27337v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27337v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27337v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

The research idea in a published paper is rarely the project's initial idea. Between the two lies a closed-loop process in which researchers run experiments, derive insights, identify challenges, explore the literature, and revise the idea, with each step motivated by evidence and reasoning they can articulate. We call the path one idea takes through this process, with the evidence and reasoning behind each step, its authentic research trajectory. Although it underlies every scientific contribution, it is rarely recorded. Existing LLM-based research agents generate an idea once, refine it against reviews, literature, or their own experimental results, or search over candidate ideas and experiments. In all of them the text of the idea evolves, while the evidence and reasoning that connected one version to the next are discarded. We propose the Research Progress Graph (RPG), a representation of authentic and complete research trajectories. An RPG records the evolution of an idea as typed research states connected by cognitive transitions, each annotated with a temporal index and the reasoning behind it, which makes this evolution an explicit object of study. We ground the representation in authentic trajectories whose steps their authors justify in annotations and papers. Comparing the representations of thirty-five research agents with the RPG, we find that each is an incomplete RPG; none keeps the state that changed, the evidence that changed it, and the reasoning connecting them as one evolving record. Modeling ideation with the RPG changes how research agents should be designed, evaluated, and trained. Agents predict the next research state instead of a complete idea, ideas are evaluated as trajectories instead of final texts, and authentic trajectories serve as training data. These directions shift research agents from generating static ideas to modeling how ideas evolve.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

These directions shift research agents from generating static ideas to modeling how ideas evolve.

</details>

## 104. When Does Hate Transfer? A Large-Scale Study of Cross-Lingual Hate Speech Detection

**中文标题**: When Does Hate Transfer? A Large-Scale Study of Cross-Lingual Hate Speech Detection

**作者**: Zahra Bokaei, Walid Magdy, Bonnie Webber

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27285v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27285v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27285v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Effective detection of hate content is essential for maintaining healthy online communities, yet performance can vary substantially across languages. This work investigates a core practical question: Can training data from one language support hate-speech detection in another language with comparable performance? To answer this question, we conduct a large-scale study across 14 languages. \change{We observe consistent patterns in which certain language pairs transfer more effectively than others. Among the factors we measure, linguistic similarity is the strongest predictor of these patterns, with temporal overlap and dataset-size difference playing smaller roles. These measurable factors do not fully explain the observed patterns; a complementary qualitative analysis suggests that shared cultural cues and communicative norms may be associated with some of the remaining cases, particularly within several clusters. We present this cultural interpretation as a hypothesis from qualitative inspection rather than a measured cause.}{} Our analysis includes low-resource languages, offering practical insights into when cross-lingual transfer can support them and where its limitations emerge. These findings inform the design of more reliable multilingual hate-detection systems.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Effective detection of hate content is essential for maintaining healthy online communities, yet performance can vary substantially across languages. This work investigates a core practical question: Can training data from one language support hate-speech detection in another language with comparable performance? To answer this question, we conduct a large-scale study across 14 languages. \change{We observe consistent patterns in which certain language pairs transfer more effectively than others. Among the factors we measure, linguistic similarity is the strongest predictor of these patterns, with temporal overlap and dataset-size difference playing smaller roles. These measurable factors do not fully explain the observed patterns; a complementary qualitative analysis suggests that shared cultural cues and communicative norms may be associated with some of the remaining cases, particularly within several clusters. We present this cultural interpretation as a hypothesis from qualitative inspection rather than a measured cause.}{} Our analysis includes low-resource languages, offering practical insights into when cross-lingual transfer can support them and where its limitations emerge. These findings inform the design of more reliable multilingual hate-detection systems.

</details>

## 105. VPFlow: Value-Predictive Planning with Imperfect World Models for Offline Goal Reaching

**中文标题**: VPFlow: Value-Predictive Planning with Imperfect World Models for Offline Goal Reaching

**作者**: Yunzhe Hu, Dong Xu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27280v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27280v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27280v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Offline goal-conditioned reinforcement learning enables skill acquisition by reaching any goal from any state without dense rewards. Model-based methods allow for planning by scaling test-time compute to solve complex tasks. Combining both holds appealing promise for pre-training on suboptimal demonstrations and zero-shot adaptation via goal specification and planning. Yet existing methods assume effective planning requires accurate next-state prediction in pixel or latent space. In this work, we show that value prediction is more useful for planning, whereas accurate state prediction is often unnecessary. Our key insight is that offline goal reaching naturally admits a closed-form Monte Carlo return, enabling value regression to replace temporal-difference bootstrapping. We use this return as the sole supervision for latent world models: imagined latents are trained to predict goal-reaching value rather than match future states, implicitly preventing representation collapse. These planning-oriented representations let imperfect world models imagine on the level sets of value field beyond dataset transitions. We then present VPFlow, a value-predictive planner that pairs the world models with a one-step flow policy and plans in its noise space via evolutionary search. It enables policy improvement with imagined generalized advantage and reduces out-of-distribution queries common in action-space planning. On 30 state and pixel OGBench datasets, VPFlow surpasses model-free baselines by at least 30.3 and 10.1 percentage points in success rate, respectively, and outperforms latent planners LeWorldModel, PLDM, and TD-MPC2. It achieves this despite a latent prediction error orders of magnitude larger than with state prediction, while minimizing this error lowers success rate. This demonstrates that value-predictive representations, not exact state prediction, are what offline planning requires.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Offline goal-conditioned reinforcement learning enables skill acquisition by reaching any goal from any state without dense rewards. Model-based methods allow for planning by scaling test-time compute to solve complex tasks. Combining both holds appealing promise for pre-training on suboptimal demonstrations and zero-shot adaptation via goal specification and planning. These planning-oriented representations let imperfect world models imagine on the level sets of value field beyond dataset transitions. It enables policy improvement with imagined generalized advantage and reduces out-of-distribution queries common in action-space planning. </details>

## 106. TxnQA: Benchmarking Analytical Reasoning and Evidence Grounding over Transaction Event Sequences

**中文标题**: TxnQA: Benchmarking Analytical Reasoning and Evidence Grounding over Transaction Event Sequences

**作者**: Rabimba Karanjai, Yiming Bian, Yun-Shiuan Chuang, Jenna L. Ballard, Zijie Pan et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27256v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27256v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27256v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Analyzing merchant transaction histories requires dynamic temporal filtering, entity resolution, and aggregation over irregular event streams--operations poorly captured by static tabular or time-series forecasting benchmarks. We introduce TxnQA, an analytical QA benchmark comprising 27 task families (81 templates) alongside a 5-scenario fraud-risk reasoning suite that evaluates both verdicts and supporting evidence over synthetic, privacy-mitigated transaction data. Ground-truth answers are computed programmatically, and model performance is decomposed into format-following rate (FFR), conditional content accuracy (CA), and end-to-end correctness (E2E). Evaluating prompted proprietary baselines and six LoRA-adapted open-weight models shows that adaptation reliably enforces output schemas but leaves arithmetic bottlenecks: Gemma-4-31B-IT and Qwen3-8B attain E2E scores of 0.867 and 0.816, whereas evaluated MoE checkpoints reach only 0.632-0.713. Furthermore, on the 500-instance fraud suite, verdict accuracy frequently exceeds 80%, yet joint accuracy (requiring exact evidence attribution) collapses to at most ~23%. These findings identify schema compliance, semantic calculation, and evidence attribution as three distinct bottlenecks in transaction-sequence QA.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce TxnQA, an analytical QA benchmark comprising 27 task families (81 templates) alongside a 5-scenario fraud-risk reasoning suite that evaluates both verdicts and supporting evidence over synthetic, privacy-mitigated transaction data. Ground-truth answers are computed programmatically, and model performance is decomposed into format-following rate (FFR), conditional content accuracy (CA), and end-to-end correctness (E2E). Evaluating prompted proprietary baselines and six LoRA-adapted open-weight models shows that adaptation reliably enforces output schemas but leaves arithmetic bottlenecks: Gemma-4-31B-IT and Qwen3-8B attain E2E scores of 0.867 and 0.816, whereas evaluated MoE checkpoints reach only 0.632-0.713. Furthermore, on the 500-instance fraud suite, verdict accuracy frequently exceeds 80%, yet joint accuracy (requiring exact evidence attribution) collapses to at most ~23%. These findings identify schema compliance, semantic calculation, and evidence attribution as three distinct bottlenecks in transaction-sequence QA.

</details>

## 107. Representing short-term movement to reveal animal identity and learning progress

**中文标题**: Representing short-term movement to reveal animal identity and learning progress

**作者**: Ping Zhang, Quang-Huy Nguyen, Yingtie Lei, Sameera Shridhar, Zachary Leveroni et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27140v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27140v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27140v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/pizhn/TRAIL.

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI, q-bio.NC

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

How learning progresses over time and varies across individuals is a longstanding question in cognitive and behavioral science. However, a major challenge is identifying observable behavioral measures that faithfully capture learning progression. Existing approaches commonly rely on predefined behavioral features or task-level performance, while fine-scale posture and movement in freely behaving animals remain less explored. In this paper, we ask: can short-term movement trajectories reveal learning progress and individual identity? We introduce a self-supervised representation learning framework built on a simple hypothesis: behavioral characteristics associated with identity and learning remain relatively stable over nearby time intervals. Using temporal proximity as supervision, our framework learns compact representations directly from movement trajectories without manually defining behavioral states or kinematic features. Using behavior data from freely behaving rats, we show that the learned representations not only improve prediction of identity and learning-related readouts over existing methods, but also provide a tool for investigating broader scientific questions about when and how these signals appear in behavior. Specifically, trajectories as short as one second contain informative learning and identity signals. Egocentric pose dynamics are more predictive of identity, whereas allocentric motion is more predictive of learning-related variation, and both signals are strongest at decision junctions in the alternation cohort. We further use a representation-conditioned diffusion model to inspect movement patterns associated with identity and learning progress. Our findings establish short-term movement as an informative behavioral "fingerprint" for studying learning and individual differences. Our code is available at https://github.com/pizhn/TRAIL.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Specifically, trajectories as short as one second contain informative learning and identity signals. Egocentric pose dynamics are more predictive of identity, whereas allocentric motion is more predictive of learning-related variation, and both signals are strongest at decision junctions in the alternation cohort. We further use a representation-conditioned diffusion model to inspect movement patterns associated with identity and learning progress. Our findings establish short-term movement as an informative behavioral "fingerprint" for studying learning and individual differences. Our code is available at https://github.com/pizhn/TRAIL.

</details>

## 108. CoopUQ: Robust Multi-Robot Social Navigation via Uncertainty-Aware Cooperative Forecasting

**中文标题**: CoopUQ: Robust Multi-Robot Social Navigation via Uncertainty-Aware Cooperative Forecasting

**作者**: Zehao Wang, Jianpeng Yao, Yuping Wang, Yiping Li, Jiaqi Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26852v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26852v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26852v1)

**项目主页**: https://coopuq.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reinforcement learning enables mobile robots to make fast and scalable navigation decisions in constrained human-shared environments. However, directly extending single-robot approaches to multi-robot settings often fails to ensure safety and efficiency, especially under occlusions and out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios, where individual robots may overlook potential risks or make inaccurate predictions. Leveraging cooperation effectively for both prediction and navigation remains a major challenge. We propose CoopUQ, a multi-robot social navigation framework that bridges uncertainty-aware cooperative occupancy forecasting and robust decision making. CoopUQ predicts future occupancy grid maps through cross-robot spatiotemporal feature fusion and further leverages forecasts and their uncertainty to guide policy via constrained multi-agent reinforcement learning, where the cost penalizes navigation through regions predicted to be occupied or highly uncertain. This design enables the policy to adapt its risk sensitivity under distribution shifts. Experimental results across multiple Unity3D-based simulation environments with both in-distribution and OOD settings, including diverse layouts, pedestrian densities, and rushing behavior, demonstrate improved prediction performance and safety over representative baselines. Real-robot experiments further demonstrate its robustness across various scenes. Our code and videos are available at https://coopuq.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reinforcement learning enables mobile robots to make fast and scalable navigation decisions in constrained human-shared environments. However, directly extending single-robot approaches to multi-robot settings often fails to ensure safety and efficiency, especially under occlusions and out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios, where individual robots may overlook potential risks or make inaccurate predictions. Leveraging cooperation effectively for both prediction and navigation remains a major challenge. We propose CoopUQ, a multi-robot social navigation framework that bridges uncertainty-aware cooperative occupancy forecasting and robust decision making. CoopUQ predicts future occupancy grid maps through cross-robot spatiotemporal feature fusion and further leverages forecasts and their uncertainty to guide policy via constrained multi-agent reinforcement learning, where the cost penalizes navigation through regions predicted to be occupied or highly uncertain. This design enables the policy to adapt its risk sensitivity under distribution shifts. Experimental results across multiple Unity3D-based simulation environments with both in-distribution and OOD settings, including diverse layouts, pedestrian densities, and rushing behavior, demonstrate improved prediction performance and safety over representative baselines. Real-robot experiments further demonstrate its robustness across various scenes. Our code and videos are available at https://coopuq.github.io/.

</details>

## 109. Next Best Action: Causal Constrained Offline RL for Business Action Targeting

**中文标题**: Next Best Action: Causal Constrained Offline RL for Business Action Targeting

**作者**: Surya Ramachandiran, Yiming Li, Juan Mancilla-Caceres, Tao Ye, Jun Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26817v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26817v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26817v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI, cs.IR, math.OC

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Next Best Action (NBA) systems must select among customer interventions (e.g., discounts, promotions, and lifecycle actions), maximize long-term economic value, satisfy complex operational constraints, and remain causally grounded. Existing approaches lack a rigorous framework for meeting these requirements jointly. We address this gap by formulating NBA as a Constrained Markov Decision Process (CMDP). We then integrate off-policy causal correction of the estimated customer dynamics directly into the Linear Programming (LP) formulation of the CMDP. The resulting policy selects actions based on customer states to maximize long-term value while explicitly enforcing business guardrails. Furthermore, the LP formulation yields shadow prices that quantify the economic trade-offs of operational constraints (e.g., fairness and user welfare), enhancing policy interpretability. We validate our proposed offline Reinforcement Learning (RL) framework on an industry case study involving rideshare coupon targeting, where customer states are defined by Recency, Frequency, and Monetary (RFM) stages. We evaluate performance using model-based off-policy evaluation, with causal dynamics estimated from a temporal holdout. Under the observed coupon budget and RFM state representation, our optimized policy improves customer lifetime value (LTV) by 19% over the estimated behavior policy and 8% over a myopic baseline. Although the demonstrated action space is binary, the proposed framework is action-agnostic, yielding a general-purpose decision module for targeting systems that manage multiple interventions.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Next Best Action (NBA) systems must select among customer interventions (e.g., discounts, promotions, and lifecycle actions), maximize long-term economic value, satisfy complex operational constraints, and remain causally grounded. Existing approaches lack a rigorous framework for meeting these requirements jointly. We address this gap by formulating NBA as a Constrained Markov Decision Process (CMDP). We then integrate off-policy causal correction of the estimated customer dynamics directly into the Linear Programming (LP) formulation of the CMDP. The resulting policy selects actions based on customer states to maximize long-term value while explicitly enforcing business guardrails. Furthermore, the LP formulation yields shadow prices that quantify the economic trade-offs of operational constraints (e.g., fairness and user welfare), enhancing policy interpretability. We validate our proposed offline Reinforcement Learning (RL) framework on an industry case study involving rideshare coupon targeting, where customer states are defined by Recency, Frequency, and Monetary (RFM) stages. We evaluate performance using model-based off-policy evaluation, with causal dynamics estimated from a temporal holdout. Under the observed coupon budget and RFM state representation, our optimized policy improves customer lifetime value (LTV) by 19% over the estimated behavior policy and 8% over a myopic baseline. Although the demonstrated action space is binary, the proposed framework is action-agnostic, yielding a general-purpose decision module for targeting systems that manage multiple interventions.

</details>

## 110. Temporal Dynamics and Security Assessment of SRT Protocol: A 96-Day Longitudinal Analysis of Production Streaming Infrastructure

**中文标题**: Temporal Dynamics and Security Assessment of SRT Protocol: A 96-Day Longitudinal Analysis of Production Streaming Infrastructure

**作者**: Ahmed F. K. Koysha, Ferdi Sönmez

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26402v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26402v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26402v1)

**类别**: cs.CR, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This study presents a 96-day longitudinal analysis of Secure Reliable Transport (SRT) protocol behavior in a production streaming environment between Iraq and Turkey, examining 262,672 transport metric samples and 5,033,104 system resource measurements collected between October 2025 and January 2026. The research addresses temporal patterns, system-resource relationships, attack vulnerability, and anomaly detection. Results reveal significant temporal variation: Negative Acknowledgment exhibits 7.6-fold hourly variation and 11.5-fold daily variation (p < 0.001), though with small effect sizes (eta^2 < 0.04), indicating that temporal period explains less than 4 percent of total variance. Central Processing Unit (CPU) utilization emerges as the strongest system-level correlate of transport variation (r = 0.617 with NAK rate). Controlled User Datagram Protocol (UDP) flood experiments at 500 packets per second cause CPU increases of 176 to 806 percent, while SRT transport metrics change by only plus or minus 13 percent demonstrating protocol resilience through the Automatic Repeat Request mechanism. An unexpected anomaly reaching 97.7 percent CPU enabled signature-based differentiation between attack types. A Z-score anomaly detection method outperforms fixed thresholds, detecting all 7 security events. This study contributes the longest temporal characterization of SRT metrics reported to date, the first system-SRT correlation analysis, and the first controlled security assessment of SRT infrastructure. These findings provide actionable guidance for capacity planning, security hardening, and multi-metric monitoring of production SRT deployments.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The research addresses temporal patterns, system-resource relationships, attack vulnerability, and anomaly detection. Results reveal significant temporal variation: Negative Acknowledgment exhibits 7.6-fold hourly variation and 11.5-fold daily variation (p < 0.001), though with small effect sizes (eta^2 < 0.04), indicating that temporal period explains less than 4 percent of total variance. Central Processing Unit (CPU) utilization emerges as the strongest system-level correlate of transport variation (r = 0.617 with NAK rate). Controlled User Datagram Protocol (UDP) flood experiments at 500 packets per second cause CPU increases of 176 to 806 percent, while SRT transport metrics change by only plus or minus 13 percent demonstrating protocol resilience through the Automatic Repeat Request mechanism. An unexpected anomaly reaching 97.7 percent CPU enabled signature-based differentiation between attack types. A Z-score anomaly detection method outperforms fixed thresholds, detecting all 7 security events. This study contributes the longest temporal characterization of SRT metrics reported to date, the first system-SRT correlation analysis, and the first controlled security assessment of SRT infrastructure. These findings provide actionable guidance for capacity planning, security hardening, and multi-metric monitoring of production SRT deployments.

</details>

## 111. Applicability and Generalization of Audio Fingerprinting Methods to Speech

**中文标题**: Applicability and Generalization of Audio Fingerprinting Methods to Speech

**作者**: Kemal Altwlkany, Elmedin Selmanovic, Sead Delalić, Emanuel Lacic

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26112v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26112v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26112v1)

**类别**: cs.SD, cs.MM, eess.AS

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Audio fingerprinting methods identify audio based on its content, even when the content has been possibly corrupted. Despite their applicability and usage in the speech domain, research and advancements in audio fingerprinting traditionally focus on music and are almost exclusively evaluated on music. We provide insights into speech-oriented audio fingerprinting by benchmarking the best performing audio fingerprinting models on speech data while taking into consideration the effects of noise, reverberation, temporal changes, pitch modifications, and lossy compression. Our findings indicate that audio fingerprinting methods based on self-supervised learning outperform classical approaches on speech data, while the performance of these models on speech is inferior compared to the performance that they achieve on music. Results also include an analysis of false positives, showing that fingerprinting methods are more likely to match with an audio of the same speaker rather than an audio of the same text, when mismatches occur.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Audio fingerprinting methods identify audio based on its content, even when the content has been possibly corrupted. Despite their applicability and usage in the speech domain, research and advancements in audio fingerprinting traditionally focus on music and are almost exclusively evaluated on music. We provide insights into speech-oriented audio fingerprinting by benchmarking the best performing audio fingerprinting models on speech data while taking into consideration the effects of noise, reverberation, temporal changes, pitch modifications, and lossy compression. Our findings indicate that audio fingerprinting methods based on self-supervised learning outperform classical approaches on speech data, while the performance of these models on speech is inferior compared to the performance that they achieve on music. Results also include an analysis of false positives, showing that fingerprinting methods are more likely to match with an audio of the same speaker rather than an audio of the same text, when mismatches occur.

</details>

## 112. Physical Words: A Vocabulary for Structuring Latent Reasoning in Robot Manipulation

**中文标题**: Physical Words: A Vocabulary for Structuring Latent Reasoning in Robot Manipulation

**作者**: Jiaming Liu, Xinghao Zhu, Yiming Zhang, Shanghang Zhang, Yinxi Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26854v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26854v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26854v2)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal reasoning provides robotic foundation models with predictive context needed for reliable action generation. However, language-based reasoning captures task semantics but struggles to express fine-grained physical dynamics, while latent reasoning models future state evolution but offers limited structure for organizing these dynamics into reusable reasoning primitives. To bridge this gap, we introduce PhysWord, a reasoning-before-acting foundation model built on Physical Words, which use atomic action verbs as shared semantic anchors for continuous representations of physical dynamics. Specifically, PhysWord predicts upcoming atomic action words and directly aligns their contextual hidden states with latent targets derived from the corresponding future observations. Building on these primitives, we construct a temporally structured PhysWord space with visual and optional tactile variants of each action word. Autoregressively predicting Physical Words across future timesteps captures transitions between execution stages, providing structured multimodal context for action generation. PhysWord achieves state-of-the-art success rates of 90.3\% on visuotactile UniVTAC and 99.6\% on vision-based LIBERO-Long, and outperforms competing methods on real-world tasks using bimanual grippers and dexterous hands. Further evaluations demonstrate that our structured latent reasoning framework generalizes to unseen scenarios and enables long-horizon manipulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, language-based reasoning captures task semantics but struggles to express fine-grained physical dynamics, while latent reasoning models future state evolution but offers limited structure for organizing these dynamics into reusable reasoning primitives. Specifically, PhysWord predicts upcoming atomic action words and directly aligns their contextual hidden states with latent targets derived from the corresponding future observations. Further evaluations demonstrate that our structured latent reasoning framework generalizes to unseen scenarios and enables long-horizon manipulation.

</details>

## 113. RoboValue: A Fine-Grained Sim-and-Real Benchmark for Unified Evaluation of Robotic Value Models

**中文标题**: RoboValue: A Fine-Grained Sim-and-Real Benchmark for Unified Evaluation of Robotic Value Models

**作者**: Shengbang Liu, Zhengye Du, Zhilong Wan, Honghao Su, Chenxiang Xia et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26663v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26663v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26663v1)

**项目主页**: https://robovalue-benchmark.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robotic value models assess task execution from visual observations and instructions, providing feedback for policy learning and execution monitoring. However, existing evaluations cover only selected aspects of execution understanding, leaving the reliability of value feedback insufficiently tested. We introduce RoboValue, a fine-grained sim-and-real benchmark for unified evaluation of robotic value models. Its framework evaluates four complementary capability dimensions through shared interfaces: task-state understanding, temporal progress monitoring, failure and recovery reasoning, and value consistency. Diagnostic trajectories and instruction variations test whether value judgments accurately reflect task requirements and changes throughout execution. RoboValue-Dataset covers 15 simulation and 20 real-world manipulation tasks, providing expert demonstrations for training and annotated trajectories for fine-grained evaluation. We evaluate representative robotic value models in zero-shot and one-shot settings under standard conditions and separate shifts in robot embodiment and environment. Our results reveal errors in fine-grained instruction grounding, history-dependent progress tracking, and recovery assessment, even in models with strong outcome and forward-progress scores. The same semantic subtask can also receive substantially different value gains across valid execution orders. These findings identify concrete targets for value supervision, while the dataset and public leaderboard support continued assessment of feedback reliability. The website is available at https://robovalue-benchmark.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 114. CAOS-SLAM: Class-Agnostic Object-Aware Real-Time vSLAM for Dynamic Environments

**中文标题**: CAOS-SLAM: Class-Agnostic Object-Aware Real-Time vSLAM for Dynamic Environments

**作者**: Gaia Borghi, João Carlos Virgolino Soares, Angelo Bratta, Shahab Heshmati-Alamdari, Claudio Semini

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26633v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26633v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26633v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robust visual Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (vSLAM) in dynamic environments faces challenges due to the presence of moving objects that may introduce erroneous observations, corrupt the map reconstruction and overall degrade localization accuracy. Existing vSLAM approaches often rely on predefined semantic classes or costly pixel-wise classification, limiting their robustness or applicability in real-world scenarios. This paper presents CAOS-SLAM, a dynamic vSLAM framework that extends ORB-SLAM3 with a hybrid perception pipeline to detect, track, and filter both semantically known and previously unseen moving objects in real-time. On-demand panoptic segmentation is performed for object discovery, with candidate unknown objects instantiated through depth-based clustering of unclassified regions. Between panoptic updates, persistent object tracks are updated using lightweight detection when available or temporal mask propagation based on world-frame Kalman prediction, depth consistency, and appearance similarity. The estimated object motion is then used to reject dynamic features from the SLAM pipeline. Experimental results on multiple dynamic RGB-D datasets and real-world experiments demonstrate improved localization accuracy over state-of-the-art dynamic vSLAM approaches, achieving up to 59% lower localization error in scenes characterized by unknown dynamic objects and up to 88% lower error in heavily occluded scenes, while achieving a median processing rate of 30 FPS on a laptop. Furthermore, the tracking module provides estimates of object trajectories and velocities, supporting potential future applications in perception, navigation, and motion-planning tasks in dynamic environments. Code and dataset will be released upon acceptance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robust visual Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (vSLAM) in dynamic environments faces challenges due to the presence of moving objects that may introduce erroneous observations, corrupt the map reconstruction and overall degrade localization accuracy. Existing vSLAM approaches often rely on predefined semantic classes or costly pixel-wise classification, limiting their robustness or applicability in real-world scenarios. This paper presents CAOS-SLAM, a dynamic vSLAM framework that extends ORB-SLAM3 with a hybrid perception pipeline to detect, track, and filter both semantically known and previously unseen moving objects in real-time. The estimated object motion is then used to reject dynamic features from the SLAM pipeline. Experimental results on multiple dynamic RGB-D datasets and real-world experiments demonstrate improved localization accuracy over state-of-the-art dynamic vSLAM approaches, achieving up to 59% lower localization error in scenes characterized by unknown dynamic objects and up to 88% lower error in heavily occluded scenes, while achieving a median processing rate of 30 FPS on a laptop. Furthermore, the tracking module provides estimates of object trajectories and velocities, supporting potential future applications in perception, navigation, and motion-planning tasks in dynamic environments. Code and dataset will be released upon acceptance.

</details>

## 115. ReInsVLN: Revisiting Historical Evidence for Training-Free Vision-and-Language Navigation

**中文标题**: ReInsVLN: Revisiting Historical Evidence for Training-Free Vision-and-Language Navigation

**作者**: Siwei Cheng, Hao Wu, Zitong Qi, Xinming Zhang, Haoyu Xi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26545v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26545v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26545v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-and-language navigation (VLN) relies on retaining and using relevant observations. Yet growing histories introduce redundancy, and retained information may remain underused. We introduce ReInsBench, a diagnostic benchmark that examines history utilization through controlled continuation trials. Its results show that history-review reinstruction can recover navigation failures using the existing task context. Motivated by this finding, we propose ReInsVLN, a training-free framework supporting memory construction and utilization in frozen navigators. Coverage- and Instruction-Aware Compression constructs compact visual context through temporal redundancy reduction, coverage-aware protection, and instruction-guided token selection. Forgetting-Inspired History Reminders guide navigators to revisit retained observations according to a predefined schedule, without additional model calls. On R2R Val-Unseen, ReInsVLN improves success rates by 8.48 and 3.37 percentage points over the native StreamVLN and InternVLA-N1, reaching 64.27% and 64.44%, respectively. Visual compression largely preserves navigation performance while reducing prefill latency and KV cache memory by up to 21.77% and 32.25%, respectively, on matched observation workloads. Real-world trials on humanoid and quadruped robots further support the applicability of ReInsVLN to sequential instruction following and ordinal navigation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 116. Learn the Task, Not the Schedule: Reducing Spurious Cross-Arm Coupling in Bimanual Imitation Learning

**中文标题**: Learn the Task, Not the Schedule: Reducing Spurious Cross-Arm Coupling in Bimanual Imitation Learning

**作者**: Shuyuan Hu, Deng-Ke Wu, Yifan Jin, Qianhang Luo, Mingchen Ju et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26507v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26507v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26507v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/MINT-SJTU/CTR.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

A bimanual demonstration specifies both what the robot should do and one particular schedule for when each arm should act. Standard imitation learning does not distinguish task-required cross-arm constraints from incidental scheduling choices, and may therefore exploit opposite-arm progress as a shortcut cue, creating spurious cross-arm coupling even where relative timing is irrelevant for the current task phase. We introduce Constraint-preserving Temporal Recombination (CTR), a training-time data intervention that preserves recorded arm-local sensorimotor content while retiming the corresponding segments to realize admissible schedules that preserve task-required cross-arm constraints. We use CTR to train Diffusion Policy, Action Chunking with Transformers, and $π_{0.5}$, and evaluate them on four simulated and four real-robot tasks. Across six real-robot bimanual progress-state initializations, CTR raises worst-state success from 56% for the strongest non-CTR schedule mixture to 76%. With $π_{0.5}$ on an Independent task, a 4 s pause transfers 2.56 s of delay to the opposite arm under the Concurrent schedule distribution, but only 0.25 s with CTR. On real robots, CTR violates task constraints in only 0--4% of trials, versus 36--98% for narrow-schedule baselines. Code will be released at https://github.com/MINT-SJTU/CTR.

</details>

On real robots, CTR violates task constraints in only 0--4% of trials, versus 36--98% for narrow-schedule baselines. Code will be released at https://github.com/MINT-SJTU/CTR.

</details>

## 117. K-AoI: A Spatio-Temporal Synchronization Metric for Digital Twin-based Teleoperation in Dynamic Network Environments

**中文标题**: K-AoI: A Spatio-Temporal Synchronization Metric for Digital Twin-based Teleoperation in Dynamic Network Environments

**作者**: Lu Tian, Zhenhui Yuan, Zexin Deng, Wenjuan Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26201v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26201v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26201v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/luziyaotian/kaoi-testbed.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.NI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Synchronization between a digital twin (DT) and its physical robot is critical for effective teleoperation, as mismatches directly degrade operator awareness and task performance. Existing synchronization metrics are predominantly time-based and rarely capture the spatial consequences of delayed information during motion. In this paper, we introduce Kinematic Age of Information (K-AoI), a spatio-temporal metric that weights AoI by instantaneous Cartesian velocity, translating temporal staleness into physically meaningful spatial divergence. We implement and evaluate K-AoI on a DT teleoperation testbed comprising a six-degrees-of-freedom (6-DOF) manipulator, a Unity-based twin, and controlled network impairments using tc netem across seven network conditions. Per-sample analysis shows that K-AoI exhibits rank-correlations with AoI at $ρ\leq 0.65$ across all networks, dropping to $ρ= 0.22$ under satellite connections. This shows that K-AoI is not a simple transformation of AoI, but instead captures velocity-dependent degradation effects that time-only metrics cannot resolve. We further analyze AoI tail behavior using Ultra-Low AoI (ULAoI) and find that the commonly assumed Generalized Pareto Distribution (GPD) model is rejected. This reflects the impact of deterministic forwarding and bounded jitter, which violate standard extreme-value assumptions. Overall, K-AoI provides a complementary and physically grounded synchronization metric, linking network delay to task-relevant spatial error in digital-twin teleoperation systems. The dataset and testbed software are publicly available at: https://github.com/luziyaotian/kaoi-testbed.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Per-sample analysis shows that K-AoI exhibits rank-correlations with AoI at $ρ\leq 0.65$ across all networks, dropping to $ρ= 0.22$ under satellite connections. This shows that K-AoI is not a simple transformation of AoI, but instead captures velocity-dependent degradation effects that time-only metrics cannot resolve. This reflects the impact of deterministic forwarding and bounded jitter, which violate standard extreme-value assumptions. The dataset and testbed software are publicly available at: https://github.com/luziyaotian/kaoi-testbed.

</details>

## 118. Data-Efficient Learning from Demonstration for Humanoid Robots via Feature-Level Perception and Prompt-Based Object Detection

**中文标题**: Data-Efficient Learning from Demonstration for Humanoid Robots via Feature-Level Perception and Prompt-Based Object Detection

**作者**: Keyhan Rayati, AliReza Beigy, Anoosheh Saadati, Pourya Shahverdi, Mehdi Tale Masouleh et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26170v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26170v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26170v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Learning from Demonstration (LfD) allows robots to learn new skills from human examples; however, many vision-based methods require large datasets and resource-intensive end-to-end training. This paper introduces a data-efficient LfD framework for humanoid robots that learns manipulation skills from compact, task-specific features instead of raw images. The proposed approach integrates layered visual preprocessing, Kinect-based human joint estimation, prompt-based object detection, and depth information to extract human motion features and three-dimensional object locations from demonstration videos. These features are used to train a temporal deep learning model that predicts robot joint-angle trajectories for execution. The framework is implemented on a NAO humanoid robot, evaluated quantitatively in simulation, and demonstrated on physical hardware. In simulation, the robot achieves a 78% success rate on a pick-and-place task using only 16 demonstrations, which increases to 86% when trained with 100 demonstrations. The framework also achieves a 71% success rate on a pushing task using eight demonstrations, highlighting its effectiveness for data-efficient humanoid skill acquisition.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The proposed approach integrates layered visual preprocessing, Kinect-based human joint estimation, prompt-based object detection, and depth information to extract human motion features and three-dimensional object locations from demonstration videos. These features are used to train a temporal deep learning model that predicts robot joint-angle trajectories for execution. The framework is implemented on a NAO humanoid robot, evaluated quantitatively in simulation, and demonstrated on physical hardware. In simulation, the robot achieves a 78% success rate on a pick-and-place task using only 16 demonstrations, which increases to 86% when trained with 100 demonstrations. The framework also achieves a 71% success rate on a pushing task using eight demonstrations, highlighting its effectiveness for data-efficient humanoid skill acquisition.

</details>

## 119. SkateNav: Learning Energy-Efficient Navigation through Adaptive Skating on Passive-Wheel Quadrupedal Robots

**中文标题**: SkateNav: Learning Energy-Efficient Navigation through Adaptive Skating on Passive-Wheel Quadrupedal Robots

**作者**: Jeil Jeong, Taeyun Kim, Sangjin Kim, Jinyoung Choi, Sung-Eui Yoon

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25962v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25962v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25962v1)

**项目主页**: https://ktw1404.github.io/SkateNav/

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Passive-wheel quadrupedal robots offer energy-efficient locomotion through leg-driven propulsion and momentum-driven gliding. However, navigation requires coordinating steering with intermittent propulsion and adapting push-glide timing to task demands. Velocity-tracking objectives may suppress the speed variations inherent in skating, discouraging efficient gliding. To address these challenges, we propose SkateNav, a unified skating-navigation framework based on reinforcement learning. A time-budgeted goal-reaching objective allows the policy to jointly learn steering, propulsion, and their temporal allocation. An adaptive skating cycle structures gliding, pushing, and recovery, while frequency modulation at every control step adjusts phase durations to navigation demands. Simulation experiments demonstrate higher average success rates and lower cost of transport than the evaluated planner-controller baselines, with ablations confirming the contributions of cycle structure and frequency adaptation. Real-world experiments demonstrate zero-shot transfer from simulation to a passive-wheel Mini Cheetah across cluttered indoor spaces, slopes, and paved outdoor surfaces. Supplementary videos are available on the project page: https://ktw1404.github.io/SkateNav/

</details>

<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 120. PaceVLA: Adaptive Temporal Action Quantization in Vision-Language-Action Models

**中文标题**: PaceVLA: Adaptive Temporal Action Quantization in Vision-Language-Action Models

**作者**: Hojin Jeon, Jimin Lee, Taekwan Kim, Nayoung Oh, Taeyoung Kim et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25961v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25961v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25961v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Fast and reliable manipulation is crucial for practical robot deployment. Although Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have recently shown remarkable progress as generalist robot policies, their execution often remains temporally inefficient. A key source of this inefficiency is that policies learn fine-grained temporal patterns from teleoperated demonstrations, requiring many low-level action steps. Temporal action quantization offers a simple way to accelerate execution by aggregating consecutive low-level actions into temporally coarser actions. However, applying action quantization without considering when fine-grained control is required introduces a speed-accuracy trade-off: accelerated actions may efficiently reduce redundant steps in some phases, while discarding critical motion details in precision-sensitive interactions. To address this, we propose PaceVLA, a unified framework for adaptive temporal execution in VLAs that integrates two coupled components: (1) mode-specific action decoders built on a shared action expert, supporting multiple temporal granularities and prediction horizons, and (2) a quantizability-guided mode selection scheme which decides whether coarse execution is appropriate, and a context-conditioned router that selects an execution mode at each inference step. Experiments across three multi-task simulation benchmarks and six real-world tasks show that PaceVLA improves execution efficiency while maintaining task success rate across multiple embodiments, including long-horizon and dexterous manipulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

A key source of this inefficiency is that policies learn fine-grained temporal patterns from teleoperated demonstrations, requiring many low-level action steps. However, applying action quantization without considering when fine-grained control is required introduces a speed-accuracy trade-off: accelerated actions may efficiently reduce redundant steps in some phases, while discarding critical motion details in precision-sensitive interactions. To address this, we propose PaceVLA, a unified framework for adaptive temporal execution in VLAs that integrates two coupled components: (1) mode-specific action decoders built on a shared action expert, supporting multiple temporal granularities and prediction horizons, and (2) a quantizability-guided mode selection scheme which decides whether coarse execution is appropriate, and a context-conditioned router that selects an execution mode at each inference step. Experiments across three multi-task simulation benchmarks and six real-world tasks show that PaceVLA improves execution efficiency while maintaining task success rate across multiple embodiments, including long-horizon and dexterous manipulation.

</details>
