## 121. TrajWAM: An Efficient Trajectory-Guided World Action Model for Dynamic Robotic Manipulation

**中文标题**: TrajWAM: An Efficient Trajectory-Guided World Action Model for Dynamic Robotic Manipulation

**作者**: Haohan Geng, Sha Zhang, Weishuo Sun, Guanting Ye, Wenhao Yu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25763v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25763v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25763v1)

**项目主页**: https://kevingeng3.github.io/TrajWAM/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic object manipulation requires robots to anticipate target motion while replanning fast enough to avoid executing outdated actions. Existing approaches either rely on fast reactive control without an explicit future target state or encode anticipated motion only implicitly. World Action Models (WAMs) offer a promising alternative by coupling future visual prediction with action generation, but scene-level video objectives may underrepresent target dynamics that are critical for manipulation, while iterative video-action denoising introduces substantial latency. We present TrajWAM, a trajectory-guided world action model for efficient dynamic robotic manipulation. Our key insight is to explicitly ground future visual imagination in predicted target-object motion and use the resulting future representation to condition action generation, while preserving this anticipation pathway after acceleration. Given a short history of object masks, a history-conditioned motion predictor forecasts future masks encoding the target object's trajectory. A lightweight Trajectory ControlNet then steers a pretrained video model toward trajectory-consistent futures. The resulting future representation conditions an action expert to predict executable action chunks. To enable one-step inference without breaking this interface, we introduce a decoupled distillation scheme that combines distribution matching for the video backbone, Trajectory ControlNet realignment, and action rollout distillation to support accurate one-step action prediction conditioned on the compressed future representation. Comprehensive evaluations in simulation and the real world show that TrajWAM achieves state-of-the-art performance on dynamic object manipulation tasks while enabling efficient one-step video-action inference. Project page: https://kevingeng3.github.io/TrajWAM/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Comprehensive evaluations in simulation and the real world show that TrajWAM achieves state-of-the-art performance on dynamic object manipulation tasks while enabling efficient one-step video-action inference. Project page: https://kevingeng3.github.io/TrajWAM/.

</details>

## 122. SteerAct: Unlocking Action Models through Few-Shot Temporal Steering

**中文标题**: SteerAct: Unlocking Action Models through Few-Shot Temporal Steering

**作者**: Kaichen Zhou, Zhiyan Li, Chongkai Gao, Yuhan Wang, Wenlong Huang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25632v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25632v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25632v1)

**项目主页**: https://steeract.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Improving pretrained robot action models at test time from only a few rollouts remains challenging. Activation steering offers a lightweight approach by modifying feature activations while keeping model weights fixed. However, effective steering requires identifying which features to modify and determining the timing and strength of modification. We introduce SteerAct, a few-shot temporal steering framework that uses a shared temporal descriptor of sparse-feature dynamics for offline feature selection and online control. A pretrained sparse autoencoder provides candidate features, and our temporal descriptor summarizes the distribution of local activation-order patterns for each feature, enabling comparisons across rollouts without requiring timestep alignment. Before steering begins (offline), we use successful and failed reference rollouts to select a single task-specific feature whose temporal descriptors best distinguish the two outcomes and construct its reference banks. During each new execution (online), we compare the feature's evolving temporal descriptor with these references to determine when to intervene and how strongly to increase or decrease its activation via a steering coefficient. Using only 24 outcome-labeled rollouts per task, SteerAct improves task success by approximately 10% relative to unsteered models in evaluations spanning $π_{0.5}$, Fast-WAM, RoboTwin 2.0, DexJoCo, and real-world manipulation, without updating action-model weights. Code and demos are available at: https://steeract.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

During each new execution (online), we compare the feature's evolving temporal descriptor with these references to determine when to intervene and how strongly to increase or decrease its activation via a steering coefficient. Using only 24 outcome-labeled rollouts per task, SteerAct improves task success by approximately 10% relative to unsteered models in evaluations spanning $π_{0.5}$, Fast-WAM, RoboTwin 2.0, DexJoCo, and real-world manipulation, without updating action-model weights. Code and demos are available at: https://steeract.github.io/.

</details>

## 123. A Hybrid TFT-NBEATS Framework for Robust Multi-Horizon Photovoltaic Power Forecasting

**中文标题**: A Hybrid TFT-NBEATS Framework for Robust Multi-Horizon Photovoltaic Power Forecasting

**作者**: Shafi Bin Sultan, Sabik Bin Sultan, Mohammad Tajrian Arafat Alvi, Rayhanul Islam Sony, Abdulah Al Numan

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27155v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27155v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27155v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Accurate multi-horizon photovoltaic (PV) power forecasting is essential for grid stability, energy scheduling, and the efficient operation of PVbatterygrid systems. However, existing models often fail to jointly capture short-term variability and long-term temporal patterns, while lacking unified multi-horizon capability and interpretability. To address these limitations, this study proposes a novel hybrid deep learning framework that integrates a Temporal Fusion Transformer (TFT) and N-BEATS within an attention-based fusion architecture. The proposed model leverages TFT to learn short-term temporal dependencies and dynamic feature importance, while N-BEATS captures long-term trend and seasonal components through interpretable basis decomposition. An adaptive attention mechanism dynamically fuses both branches, enabling balanced multi-scale learning. The framework simultaneously generates forecasts for 30-minute, 2-hour, 6-hour, 12-hour, and 24-hour horizons within a single unified pipeline. The model is evaluated using multi-year real-world datasets from the Desert Knowledge Australia Solar Centre (DKASC). Experimental results demonstrate superior performance over statistical, machine learning, and deep learning baselines. The proposed model achieves RMSE values of 42.35, 46.27, 52.29, and 48.11 across the 20162017, 2023, 2024, and 20252026 datasets, respectively, corresponding to a 3540% reduction compared to the best baseline (TFT). Additionally, MAPE remains below 5% across all datasets, indicating high predictive accuracy and stability. The model also provides interpretability through attention visualization and trendseasonality decomposition, and is deployed on a cloud-based platform for real-time forecasting. These results demonstrate that the proposed framework is robust, interpretable, and suitable for practical renewable energy applications.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 124. Graph Neural Networks for Astrophysics and Cosmology

**中文标题**: Graph Neural Networks for Astrophysics and Cosmology

**作者**: Christian Kragh Jespersen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27051v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27051v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27051v1)

**类别**: astro-ph.IM, astro-ph.CO, astro-ph.GA, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Physics seldom makes breakthroughs when we consider objects only in isolation. Instead we model and measure how objects sit among their neighbours, spatial, temporal, or otherwise: a galaxy in its environment, a halo in its history, a photon hit in a detector. Graphs are ideally suited for encoding exactly these relations, and Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) learn the geometric functions that respect them. This review presents the use of GNNs for astrophysics and cosmology, and argues that GNNs often suit physics and physical data better than any alternative: they fit the native geometry of the domain and the signal, enable the incorporation of the symmetries and prior knowledge we already trust and have toiled to build, and stay flexible enough to capture what we do not yet understand. GNNs' physically intuitive workings also render them more interpretable than other deep-learning architectures. We show how we can begin from general principles of the geometry of deep learning and build the graph and the graph neural network from the ground up, and how other architectures, like the convolutional network and the transformer, can be seen as special cases of one unified geometric blueprint. We survey applications across cosmology, galaxy formation, dynamics, and instrumentation; and we chart the frontier. From this survey, we find that only one in six articles outside instrumentation applies GNNs to data rather than just simulations. We also distil a set of best practices, including when not to reach for a graph. Throughout, we will prioritise physical intuition and foundational principles over mathematical machinery whenever possible.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Physics seldom makes breakthroughs when we consider objects only in isolation. Instead we model and measure how objects sit among their neighbours, spatial, temporal, or otherwise: a galaxy in its environment, a halo in its history, a photon hit in a detector. Graphs are ideally suited for encoding exactly these relations, and Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) learn the geometric functions that respect them. This review presents the use of GNNs for astrophysics and cosmology, and argues that GNNs often suit physics and physical data better than any alternative: they fit the native geometry of the domain and the signal, enable the incorporation of the symmetries and prior knowledge we already trust and have toiled to build, and stay flexible enough to capture what we do not yet understand. GNNs' physically intuitive workings also render them more interpretable than other deep-learning architectures. We show how we can begin from general principles of the geometry of deep learning and build the graph and the graph neural network from the ground up, and how other architectures, like the convolutional network and the transformer, can be seen as special cases of one unified geometric blueprint. We survey applications across cosmology, galaxy formation, dynamics, and instrumentation; and we chart the frontier. From this survey, we find that only one in six articles outside instrumentation applies GNNs to data rather than just simulations. We also distil a set of best practices, including when not to reach for a graph. Throughout, we will prioritise physical intuition and foundational principles over mathematical machinery whenever possible.

</details>

## 125. Global Time-Aware Conditioning (GTC): A Mechanism for Time-Aware Pediatric Sleep Representation Learning

**中文标题**: Global Time-Aware Conditioning (GTC): A Mechanism for Time-Aware Pediatric Sleep Representation Learning

**作者**: Saurav Raj Pandey, Harlin Lee

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26618v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26618v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26618v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Sleep physiology evolves throughout the night, with changes in sleep cycles, respiratory stability, and cardiovascular dynamics. However, self-supervised learning (SSL) methods for sleep typically process short, fixed-duration windows without explicitly modeling where each window occurs within the full sleep session. We introduce Global Time-Aware Conditioning (GTC), a mechanism that complements local positional encodings by conditioning latent representations on the global time-of-sleep of each input window. We integrate GTC into three representative SSL frameworks and evaluate them on five clinically relevant tasks with extreme class imbalance across two independent pediatric cohorts. GTC generally improves F1 and AUPRC across architectures and tasks, with gains persisting under cross-cohort transfer, highlighting the value of global temporal context for sleep representation learning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Sleep physiology evolves throughout the night, with changes in sleep cycles, respiratory stability, and cardiovascular dynamics. However, self-supervised learning (SSL) methods for sleep typically process short, fixed-duration windows without explicitly modeling where each window occurs within the full sleep session. We introduce Global Time-Aware Conditioning (GTC), a mechanism that complements local positional encodings by conditioning latent representations on the global time-of-sleep of each input window. We integrate GTC into three representative SSL frameworks and evaluate them on five clinically relevant tasks with extreme class imbalance across two independent pediatric cohorts. GTC generally improves F1 and AUPRC across architectures and tasks, with gains persisting under cross-cohort transfer, highlighting the value of global temporal context for sleep representation learning.

</details>

## 126. A Semantic Knowledge Graph Construction Pipeline for Vehicle Intention Prediction in nuScenes

**中文标题**: A Semantic Knowledge Graph Construction Pipeline for Vehicle Intention Prediction in nuScenes

**作者**: Khaled Nowara, Mohamed Manzour, Catherine Elias

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26528v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26528v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26528v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Knowledge graphs (KGs) are increasingly used in autonomous driving (AD) to make traffic scene entities and relations explicit for prediction and reasoning. Existing traffic-scene KGs, however, primarily emphasize trajectory forecasting or general scene understanding. This paper presents the nuScenes Intention Knowledge Graph (NIKG), a focused Web Ontology Language (OWL)-based semantic representation and construction pipeline for ego-centric vehicle intention context. NIKG instantiates nuScenes scenes as a self-describing Turtle graph and promotes three intention-relevant relations to first-class properties of each scene participant: ego-relative motion, spatial configuration, and time to collision (TTC) risk. The pipeline uses a small input contract available in many ego-centric driving datasets: ego pose, tracked object annotations, and temporal keyframe order. NIKG is released as an ontology-backed, self-describing graph artifact together with a reproducible construction pipeline and validation protocol for ego-centric intention context. Applying this pipeline to nuScenes mini yields a graph of 211,335 triples across 404 keyframes; under the validation protocol, ego-relative motion labels achieve 92.5% forward consistency. Coverage, semantic consistency, and temporal coherence analyses over this artifact are further reported. NIKG occupies the space between scene-oriented traffic KGs and intention-prediction models: a small, explicit semantic layer intended to support downstream work such as knowledge graph embedding (KGE), SPARQL-based feature extraction, or hybrid neuro-symbolic reasoning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 127. Regime-Aware Interpretable Ranking for Sector Rotation

**中文标题**: Regime-Aware Interpretable Ranking for Sector Rotation

**作者**: Kelvin J. L. Koa, Ziling Liu, Xinyang Li, Shuowei Feng, Ruoyu Liang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26456v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26456v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26456v1)

**类别**: q-fin.PM, cs.LG, q-fin.CP

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Sector rotation is a common decision in benchmark-relative portfolio management, where portfolio managers rank sectors for overweight and underweight positions. We formulate this task as ranking sector actions by expected future risk-adjusted active performance, measured with forward sector-benchmark Sharpe ratios. Such rankings should preserve temporal information, express model outputs through economically familiar signal characteristics, account for changes in signal relevance across macroeconomic environments, and capture dependencies among sectors. We propose RegimeRank, an interpretable sector-ranking model that represents fundamental and technical signals through expert-defined temporal characteristics, conditions them on learned macro factors via cross-attention, and models cross-sector dependencies through self-attention. Expected-gradient attributions identify the contributions of individual signal-transformation pairs, while macroeconomic and cross-sector attention maps provide diagnostics of the learned conditioning and relational structure. We evaluate RegimeRank on weekly S&P 500 data from 2002 to 2024 under combinatorial purged cross-validation, where it outperforms representative ranking and machine-learning baselines across multiple ranking metrics. Ablation studies support the contributions of latent macro states, regime conditioning, and cross-sector modelling, while attribution and attention analyses illustrate the temporal characteristics, macro states, and sector relationships underlying its rankings.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Such rankings should preserve temporal information, express model outputs through economically familiar signal characteristics, account for changes in signal relevance across macroeconomic environments, and capture dependencies among sectors. We propose RegimeRank, an interpretable sector-ranking model that represents fundamental and technical signals through expert-defined temporal characteristics, conditions them on learned macro factors via cross-attention, and models cross-sector dependencies through self-attention. Expected-gradient attributions identify the contributions of individual signal-transformation pairs, while macroeconomic and cross-sector attention maps provide diagnostics of the learned conditioning and relational structure. We evaluate RegimeRank on weekly S&P 500 data from 2002 to 2024 under combinatorial purged cross-validation, where it outperforms representative ranking and machine-learning baselines across multiple ranking metrics. Ablation studies support the contributions of latent macro states, regime conditioning, and cross-sector modelling, while attribution and attention analyses illustrate the temporal characteristics, macro states, and sector relationships underlying its rankings.

</details>

## 128. CTLite: Lightweight Irregular Multivariate Time Series Forecasting with Reusable Continuous-Time Summaries

**中文标题**: CTLite: Lightweight Irregular Multivariate Time Series Forecasting with Reusable Continuous-Time Summaries

**作者**: Shusen Ma, Xudong Zuo, Yangchun Xie, Yun-Bo Zhao

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26379v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26379v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26379v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/SSMa913/NICLab-CTLite.

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Irregular multivariate time series forecasting (IMTSF) requires capturing temporal dynamics and cross-variable dependencies from sparse, asynchronous observations. Existing methods often rely on learned temporal encoders, coupling historical representation construction with model optimisation and repeatedly processing the same observations. We propose CTLite, a lightweight forecasting framework that decouples historical summarisation from learned prediction. Specifically, a parameter-free continuous-time moment encoder summarises local levels and trends at multiple temporal scales while suppressing poorly supported slope estimates. A compact readout combines damped local extrapolation with a low-rank cross-variable correction that accounts for observation freshness, target availability, and forecast horizon. Since the historical summaries are independent of trainable parameters and forecast queries, they can be reused across optimisation steps and repeated predictions for an unchanged history without altering predictions or parameter gradients. Experiments on four real-world benchmarks show that CTLite achieves the best or second-best mean MSE and MAE among the evaluated methods with only 88-2,537 trainable parameters. Under matched GPU workloads at batch size 32, CTLite reduces inference latency by 18.6-87.3% compared with the latest models, including the cost of historical encoding. Reusing summaries further reduces total runtime by approximately 52% over 16 forecast calls on the same history compared with recomputing the encoder for every call, including the initial encoding cost. The code is at: https://github.com/SSMa913/NICLab-CTLite.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Irregular multivariate time series forecasting (IMTSF) requires capturing temporal dynamics and cross-variable dependencies from sparse, asynchronous observations. Existing methods often rely on learned temporal encoders, coupling historical representation construction with model optimisation and repeatedly processing the same observations. We propose CTLite, a lightweight forecasting framework that decouples historical summarisation from learned prediction. Specifically, a parameter-free continuous-time moment encoder summarises local levels and trends at multiple temporal scales while suppressing poorly supported slope estimates. A compact readout combines damped local extrapolation with a low-rank cross-variable correction that accounts for observation freshness, target availability, and forecast horizon. Since the historical summaries are independent of trainable parameters and forecast queries, they can be reused across optimisation steps and repeated predictions for an unchanged history without altering predictions or parameter gradients. Experiments on four real-world benchmarks show that CTLite achieves the best or second-best mean MSE and MAE among the evaluated methods with only 88-2,537 trainable parameters. Under matched GPU workloads at batch size 32, CTLite reduces inference latency by 18.6-87.3% compared with the latest models, including the cost of historical encoding. Reusing summaries further reduces total runtime by approximately 52% over 16 forecast calls on the same history compared with recomputing the encoder for every call, including the initial encoding cost. The code is at: https://github.com/SSMa913/NICLab-CTLite.

</details>

## 129. ViMoLa: Discrete Motion Tokens for Language-Guided Video Generation

**中文标题**: ViMoLa: Discrete Motion Tokens for Language-Guided Video Generation

**作者**: Bishoy Galoaa, Sarah Ostadabbas

**发布日期**: 2026-10-18

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25595v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25595v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25595v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Language-guided video generation often captures scene content while missing the requested motion. We present ViMoLa (Video Motion Language), which predicts a motion prior from language and injects it into a frozen image-to-video backbone. A spatial vector-quantized autoencoder compresses dense trajectories into 64 tokens from a 512-entry codebook; a 28M-parameter transformer predicts the tokens from text; and zero-gated cross-attention adapters condition every frozen Wan~2.2 DiT block through forward hooks. Trained on 462K videos from Something-Something~v2 and Kinetics-400, ViMoLa raises directional accuracy from 0.287 to 0.442 (chance 0.250), with similar measured generative quality. Across-prompt shuffled codes achieve 0.283, supporting the importance of prompt-matched token content. A low-rank adapter reaches 0.428 at 1.45% additional forward-pass FLOPs, and reusing the tokenizer and language model with newly trained CogVideoX adapters raises accuracy from 0.301 to 0.398. Control remains imperfect: oracle codes reach 0.531, compound-motion gains are smaller, and the adapter does not explicitly preserve token-to-patch assignments.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

A spatial vector-quantized autoencoder compresses dense trajectories into 64 tokens from a 512-entry codebook; a 28M-parameter transformer predicts the tokens from text; and zero-gated cross-attention adapters condition every frozen Wan~2.2 DiT block through forward hooks. Trained on 462K videos from Something-Something~v2 and Kinetics-400, ViMoLa raises directional accuracy from 0.287 to 0.442 (chance 0.250), with similar measured generative quality. Across-prompt shuffled codes achieve 0.283, supporting the importance of prompt-matched token content. A low-rank adapter reaches 0.428 at 1.45% additional forward-pass FLOPs, and reusing the tokenizer and language model with newly trained CogVideoX adapters raises accuracy from 0.301 to 0.398. Control remains imperfect: oracle codes reach 0.531, compound-motion gains are smaller, and the adapter does not explicitly preserve token-to-patch assignments.

</details>

## 130. Local Evidence, Global Failure: Diagnosing Speech-Vision Temporal Composition in Audio-Visual Language Models

**中文标题**: Local Evidence, Global Failure: Diagnosing Speech-Vision Temporal Composition in Audio-Visual Language Models

**作者**: Jaehong Myung, Seunghun Oh, Unsang Park

**发布日期**: 2026-10-18

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25352v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25352v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25352v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Audio-visual large language models (AV-LLMs) can fail at full-video speech-vision decisions even when useful visual evidence is locally recoverable. We introduce Temporal Evidence Decomposition (TED), a training-free diagnostic framework that separates local visual verification from speech-relative aggregation. Automatic speech recognition supplies a transcript and utterance interval. TED queries audio-free windows with the transcript for Temporal Alignment and Forecasting, or answer candidates for Cross-Modal Temporal Binding, then aggregates evidence relative to that interval. Across four frozen AV-LLMs on SVHalluc, Temporal Forecasting accuracy rises from 29.3-33.7% under full-video questioning to 79.3-82.2% with TED. Query replacement reduces recovery; the observed anchor remains beneficial when temporal-region availability is held fixed. Physical-isolation and timing-shortcut controls test alternative explanations. The findings distinguish locally recoverable evidence from successful full-video decisions, motivating explicit separation of narration content, utterance timing, and time-indexed visual evidence.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Audio-visual large language models (AV-LLMs) can fail at full-video speech-vision decisions even when useful visual evidence is locally recoverable. We introduce Temporal Evidence Decomposition (TED), a training-free diagnostic framework that separates local visual verification from speech-relative aggregation. Across four frozen AV-LLMs on SVHalluc, Temporal Forecasting accuracy rises from 29.3-33.7% under full-video questioning to 79.3-82.2% with TED. ## 131. SurGPT: A Multi-Task Foundation Model for Robotic Surgical Skills Assessment and Gesture Recognition

**中文标题**: SurGPT: A Multi-Task Foundation Model for Robotic Surgical Skills Assessment and Gesture Recognition

**作者**: Parham M. Kebria, Saeid Nahavandi, Mehrdad Saif, Fernando Bello

**发布日期**: 2026-10-18

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25426v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25426v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25426v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

The assessment of surgical skills in robotic surgery training has traditionally relied on subjective evaluations by human experts, creating bottlenecks in surgical education and limiting scalability. We present a novel foundation model that automatically analyzes robotic surgical videos to provide comprehensive skill assessment and gesture understanding. Combining a visual encoder with a transformer architecture, the model performs three key tasks simultaneously: 1) surgical task classification (knot tying, needle passing, suturing), 2) skill level assessment on a standardized scale, and 3) natural language generation of surgical gesture descriptions. The model processes video sequences from the da Vinci surgical robot and generates human-readable text descriptions of surgical actions, similar to how language models generate text from prompts, using a multi-task learning framework with adaptive uncertainty weighting to optimize performance across all objectives. Our transformer-based architecture incorporates positional encoding for temporal understanding and attention mechanisms for spatiotemporal feature integration. Evaluated on the da Vinci Skills Simulator dataset, the proposed framework demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in surgical task recognition and provides interpretable skill assessments that correlate with expert evaluations, with the model's ability to generate natural language descriptions of surgical gestures opening new possibilities for automated surgical education, real-time feedback systems, and objective skill assessment in surgical training and certification programs.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The model processes video sequences from the da Vinci surgical robot and generates human-readable text descriptions of surgical actions, similar to how language models generate text from prompts, using a multi-task learning framework with adaptive uncertainty weighting to optimize performance across all objectives. Our transformer-based architecture incorporates positional encoding for temporal understanding and attention mechanisms for spatiotemporal feature integration. Evaluated on the da Vinci Skills Simulator dataset, the proposed framework demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in surgical task recognition and provides interpretable skill assessments that correlate with expert evaluations, with the model's ability to generate natural language descriptions of surgical gestures opening new possibilities for automated surgical education, real-time feedback systems, and objective skill assessment in surgical training and certification programs.

</details>

## 132. Energy-Score Stabilized Attentive Mixture-Density Policy: A Lightweight Approach to Multimodal Robot Motion

**中文标题**: Energy-Score Stabilized Attentive Mixture-Density Policy: A Lightweight Approach to Multimodal Robot Motion

**作者**: Sachiya Fujita, Hideyuki Ichiwara, Shigeki Sugano, Tetsuya Ogata

**发布日期**: 2026-10-18

**arXiv ID**: [2610.25401v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25401v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.25401v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

For practical motion generation, it is essential to handle multimodality (multiple actions can be correct for the same observation) while maintaining low inference cost and strong generalization from limited demonstrations. We introduce an attentive mixture-density policy trained with the Energy Score that represents future visuomotor trajectories as explicit probability distributions with a single forward pass. Concretely, we output Gaussian mixture parameters for joint commands (and optionally attention points) and optimize them with the Energy Score (computed as a sum of 1D CRPS under a diagonal covariance assumption) instead of the negative log-likelihood (NLL) to avoid MDN singularities and gradient-scale instabilities. Our architecture couples explicit spatial attention with mixture-density outputs, enabling robust multimodal behavior selection under visual noise and small-data regimes. On an 8-DOF manipulator, the method generates distinct avoidance trajectories and stabilizes learning in obstacle avoidance. Moreover, in a randomized moving-object grasping task, where spatiotemporal multimodality and fast timing decisions are required, the proposed model outperforms Diffusion Policy by 15-25% under several unseen conditions, demonstrating superior generalization and real-time feasibility.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

For practical motion generation, it is essential to handle multimodality (multiple actions can be correct for the same observation) while maintaining low inference cost and strong generalization from limited demonstrations. We introduce an attentive mixture-density policy trained with the Energy Score that represents future visuomotor trajectories as explicit probability distributions with a single forward pass. Concretely, we output Gaussian mixture parameters for joint commands (and optionally attention points) and optimize them with the Energy Score (computed as a sum of 1D CRPS under a diagonal covariance assumption) instead of the negative log-likelihood (NLL) to avoid MDN singularities and gradient-scale instabilities. Our architecture couples explicit spatial attention with mixture-density outputs, enabling robust multimodal behavior selection under visual noise and small-data regimes. On an 8-DOF manipulator, the method generates distinct avoidance trajectories and stabilizes learning in obstacle avoidance.
